# Settings reference

Generated by `scripts/gen_settings_docs.py` from `roxy/config/catalog.py`. Do not edit this file by hand;
change the setting's declaration and run the script again (CI runs it with `--check`).

Catalog version `8e68845ee39f6e96`, 493 settings in 14 groups.

## How to read this page

Every setting is live unless its Applies row says otherwise: a change reaches every worker within about a
second. Plain numbers for durations and sizes are in the unit shown; the editor and API also accept text
such as "90s", "15m", "2h", "64 MiB" or "1 GiB" (KB, MB and GB are powers of 1000; KiB, MiB and GiB are
powers of 1024). High-risk values need a reason and a confirmation. Dashboard lists the cards where the
setting can also be edited inline, besides the Settings page.

<a id="cross-field-rules"></a>

## Cross-field rules

A change is refused when it would break one of these (checked on save and on import):

- `adaptive_min_per_min` <= `adaptive_max_per_min`
- `aimd_min` <= `aimd_max`
- `cooldown_min_s` <= `cooldown_max_s`
- `spam_auth_ban_minutes` <= `spam_auth_ban_max_minutes`
- `spam_bust_ban_minutes` <= `spam_bust_ban_max_minutes`
- `spam_dist_ban_minutes` <= `spam_dist_ban_max_minutes`
- `spam_enum_ban_minutes` <= `spam_enum_ban_max_minutes`
- `spam_probe_ban_minutes` <= `spam_probe_ban_max_minutes`
- `spam_rate_ban_minutes` <= `spam_rate_ban_max_minutes`
- `spam_refused_ban_minutes` <= `spam_refused_ban_max_minutes`
- `tarpit_jitter_min_ms` <= `tarpit_jitter_max_ms`
- `tarpit_min_seconds` <= `tarpit_max_seconds`
- `aimd_min` <= `aimd_initial` <= `aimd_max`
- `cooldown_min_s` <= `cooldown_default_s` <= `cooldown_max_s`
- `backoff_base_ms` <= `backoff_cap_ms`
- `insight_up_latency_p95_ms` <= `insight_up_latency_p99_ms`
- `insight_cache_ttl_tune_identical_lower_pct` <= `insight_cache_ttl_tune_identical_raise_pct`
- `spam_auth_ban_minutes` >= 1 and `spam_auth_ban_max_minutes` >= 1 while `spam_auth_action` = ban
- `spam_bust_ban_minutes` >= 1 and `spam_bust_ban_max_minutes` >= 1 while `spam_bust_action` = ban
- `spam_dist_ban_minutes` >= 1 and `spam_dist_ban_max_minutes` >= 1 while `spam_dist_action` = ban
- `spam_enum_ban_minutes` >= 1 and `spam_enum_ban_max_minutes` >= 1 while `spam_enum_action` = ban
- `spam_probe_ban_minutes` >= 1 and `spam_probe_ban_max_minutes` >= 1 while `spam_probe_action` = ban
- `spam_rate_ban_minutes` >= 1 and `spam_rate_ban_max_minutes` >= 1 while `spam_rate_action` = ban
- `spam_refused_ban_minutes` >= 1 and `spam_refused_ban_max_minutes` >= 1 while `spam_refused_action` = ban
- `credential_probe_reserved_per_min` < `credential_bucket_per_min`
- `bot_score_legit_max` < `bot_score_abuse_min`
- owner deadline (`queue_wait_interactive_ms` + `request_timeout` x `upstream_max_attempts` + `backoff_cap_ms`) <= `request_deadline_s` - 2 s
- `tarpit_max_seconds` <= `request_deadline_s` - 2 s
- `cache_coalesce_wait_ms` (when not 0) <= `request_deadline_s` - 2 s
- `request_deadline_s` <= 90
- the host of `credential_probe_url` is in `allowed_roblox_hosts` when `strict_host_allowlist` = 1

## Routing and egress

### `direct_enabled`

Allows Roxy to send anonymous requests (without the Roblox login cookie) straight from the server's own IP address. This direct path is free, and it is the normal way public traffic reaches Roblox.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Direct path enabled |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 1 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Anonymous traffic leaves from the server IP, paced by the direct rate bucket. The rotator is used only when the weights, a routing rule, a direct cooldown or a nearly full direct bucket send traffic there. |
| If disabled | Every anonymous request has to go through the paid rotator, so every call costs bandwidth money. If the rotator is also off, out of budget or cooling down, callers get a 503 (egress_disabled) or a stale cached copy instead of fresh data. |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value = 0: All anonymous traffic is forced onto the paid rotator, which spends quota on every request, and if the rotator is unavailable too, Roxy cannot reach Roblox at all. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#routing` |
| Related settings | `rotator_enabled`, `direct_weight`, `rotator_weight`, `direct_bucket_per_min` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | This switch does not affect the credential path, which always leaves from the server IP and is controlled by credential_enabled. |

### `direct_weight`

The relative share of anonymous upstream calls sent from the server IP when both the direct path and the rotator are available. Weights are compared with each other, not read as percentages: direct 300 and rotator 100 means about three direct calls for every rotator call.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Direct path weight |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | int |
| Default | 100 |
| Range | 0 to 1000 |
| Unit | none |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | A larger share of anonymous traffic leaves from the server IP. That is free, but it concentrates Roblox's per-IP rate limits on one address. |
| If lowered | A larger share goes to the rotator, which is billed per byte. At 0, ordinary traffic goes to the rotator whenever it is available. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value = 0: Ordinary anonymous traffic goes to the paid rotator whenever it is available, spending quota on calls the free direct path could have made. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#routing` |
| Related settings | `rotator_weight`, `direct_shift_threshold_pct`, `direct_enabled` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `UP-429-HOST`, `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | `token_weight` |
| v1 default | 75 |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | In v1 this weight was the share of traffic that carried the operator's login cookie; v2 direct traffic is anonymous. The v1 value is never imported because its meaning changed (plan 18.3). |

### `rotator_weight`

The relative share of anonymous upstream calls sent through the rotator (DataImpulse, a paid proxy service that sends calls from many different exit IP addresses) while everything is healthy. The default 0 makes the rotator a spillover path only: it is used when the direct path is cooling down, its bucket is nearly full, or a routing rule prefers the rotator.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Rotator weight |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | int |
| Default | 0 |
| Range | 0 to 1000 |
| Unit | none |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | More calls go out through rotator IPs, which spreads load away from the server IP, but each one is billed by the byte and rotator IPs are often already rate-limited by Roblox. |
| If lowered | Less rotator spending and more load on the server IP. At 0 the rotator only takes spillover. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#routing`, `egress#rotator` |
| Related settings | `direct_weight`, `direct_shift_threshold_pct`, `rotator_enabled`, `rotator_hard_stop_pct` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `UP-429-HOST`, `UP-TIMEOUT`, `EGR-BURN`, `EGR-POOL-BURNED` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | `rotate_weight` |
| v1 default | 25 |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Owner decision D13 chose 0 on purpose, so the v1 value of 25 is never imported (plan 18.3). The monthly hard stop and the daily cap still bound spending whatever this weight is. |

### `direct_shift_threshold_pct`

How full the direct path's rate bucket may get before traffic starts moving to the rotator. Above this fill level the direct weight shifts gradually toward the rotator (only if the rotator is enabled and within budget), so traffic eases off the server IP before it reaches its pace limit.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Direct spillover threshold |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | percent |
| Default | 80 percent |
| Range | 0 to 100 percent |
| Unit | percent |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Spillover starts later: the server IP carries more traffic before any moves to the rotator. That saves money but lets the direct bucket run closer to full, so more calls wait in the queue. |
| If lowered | Spillover starts earlier: traffic moves to the rotator sooner, which keeps the direct bucket emptier but spends more rotator bytes. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#routing` |
| Related settings | `direct_weight`, `rotator_weight`, `direct_bucket_per_min`, `direct_bucket_burst` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `EGR-BURN` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | `token_danger_zone` |
| v1 default | 60 percent |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Successor of v1's token_danger_zone, which counted requests in the token window instead of measuring bucket fill. The v1 value is never imported because its meaning changed (plan 18.3). |

### `rotator_enabled`

Master switch for the rotator, the paid DataImpulse proxy pool that sends anonymous requests from many different exit IP addresses. It only works once a gateway URL is configured on the Egress page or in the bootstrap credential, and it never carries the Roblox login credential.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Rotator enabled |
| Group | Routing and egress |

| Default | 1 |

| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |

| If disabled | No rotator use and no rotator spending. When the direct path is cooling down or saturated, callers get a stale cached copy or a 429 with Retry-After instead of being served through another IP. |

| Notes | Independently of this switch, a credential leak guard trip disables the egress that tripped it until an admin re-enables it (plan C2). |

### `rotator_cooldown_s`

How long the rotator is parked (not used at all) after rotator_max_failures failures in a row. Failures are connection errors, timeouts, Roblox 429 rate-limit answers and Roblox 5xx server errors seen through the rotator.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Rotator pause after failures |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 60 seconds (1m) |
| Range | 5 to 3600 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | A broken or rate-limited pool rests longer before Roxy tries it again, so fewer bytes are wasted on it, but recovery after a short provider hiccup is slower. |
| If lowered | Roxy retries a troubled pool sooner. Brief outages recover faster, but Roxy may keep paying for calls into a pool that is still failing. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `egress#rotator` |
| Related settings | `rotator_max_failures`, `rotator_cooldown_distinct_exits`, `rotator_cooldown_window_s` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | `rotate_cooldown` |
| v1 default | 60 seconds (1m) |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

### `rotator_max_failures`

How many consecutive rotator failures park the rotator for rotator_cooldown_s. A failure is a connection error, a timeout, a Roblox 429 or a Roblox 5xx. In v1 only connection-level failures counted, so a rate-limited pool was never parked.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Rotator failures before pause |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | int |
| Default | 3 failures |
| Range | 1 to 50 failures |
| Unit | failures |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Longer failure streaks are tolerated, so a flaky pool keeps receiving traffic (and spending bytes) for longer. |
| If lowered | The rotator is parked after fewer failures. That protects the budget but can park a healthy pool after a short unlucky streak. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `egress#rotator` |
| Related settings | `rotator_cooldown_s` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |

| v1 default | 3 failures |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

### `rotator_session_mode`

How long Roxy keeps using the same rotator exit IP address. Keeping one (a sticky session) reuses its connection and saves handshake bytes; switching often spreads calls across more IPs. While rotator_session_username_template is empty, sticky modes are unavailable and Roxy uses per_request.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Rotator session mode |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | enum |
| Default | "sticky_until_429" |
| Range | one of `per_request`, `sticky`, `sticky_until_429` |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `egress#rotator` |
| Related settings | `rotator_session_username_template`, `rotator_sticky_seconds`, `rotator_max_sessions`, `rotator_tls_overhead_bytes` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `EGR-POOL-BURNED`, `UP-TIMEOUT` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | The H-ROTATOR-SESSION health check verifies that one session id keeps one exit IP and that two session ids get different IPs. |

Options:

- `per_request` (New IP every request): Every rotator call gets a fresh exit IP. Works without a session username template, but every call opens a new connection with its own encryption handshake (about 6 KB and one extra round trip), so it costs the most bytes.
- `sticky` (Sticky for a fixed time): Keeps one exit IP for rotator_sticky_seconds, then moves to a new one, even if the old one was fine. Needs rotator_session_username_template.
- `sticky_until_429` (Sticky until rate-limited): Keeps one exit IP for as long as it stays healthy and moves to a new one as soon as Roblox answers it with a 429. Reuses good IPs and drops burned ones. Needs rotator_session_username_template.

### `rotator_sticky_seconds`

How long one rotator exit IP is kept before Roxy moves to a new one. Used only when rotator_session_mode is sticky and a session username template is set.

| Field | Value |

| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Fewer new connections and handshakes (fewer bytes), but an exit IP that Roblox has started to limit stays in use longer. |
| If lowered | More IP changes, more new connections, and more encryption handshake bytes per hour. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `egress#rotator` |

| Notes | none |

### `rotator_session_username_template`

The pattern used to build the DataImpulse proxy username for one sticky session: {user} becomes the username from the stored gateway URL, {session} becomes Roxy's random session id, and {country} becomes rotator_country. Empty means sticky sessions are unavailable and every rotator call uses a new IP.

| v1 default | none |
| Max length | 200 |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | yes |
| Notes | PENDING owner verification: the syntax is defined by DataImpulse, so Roxy ships no guess. Check the provider's documentation and fill it in; the shape is something like {user}__sessid-{session}, but that is only an example. Always use the {user} placeholder and never type the real username or password here. A wrong template makes the gateway reject rotator calls, which parks the rotator; the H-ROTATOR-SESSION health check confirms a new template works. |

### `rotator_max_sessions`

How many sticky rotator sessions each worker process keeps open at once. Each session needs its own proxy connection pool, so Roxy keeps the most recently used ones and closes the oldest when the cap is reached. The cap is per worker: the server holds up to this number times the worker count.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Warm rotator sessions per worker |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | int |
| Default | 16 sessions |
| Range | 1 to 256 sessions |
| Unit | sessions |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | More exit IPs stay warm and reusable, at the cost of more open sockets and memory in every worker, and the production server has little memory to spare. |
| If lowered | Sessions are closed and reopened more often: more handshakes, more bytes, more exit IP changes. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `egress#rotator` |
| Related settings | `rotator_session_mode`, `rotator_sticky_seconds` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

### `rotator_cooldown_distinct_exits`

How many different rotator exit IPs must get a Roblox 429 on the same endpoint within rotator_cooldown_window_s before Roxy stops using the rotator for that endpoint until the cooldown ends. A single 429 only swaps that session for a new exit, because one burned IP says little about the rest of the pool.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Rate-limited exits before endpoint pause |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | int |
| Default | 3 exits |
| Range | 1 to 20 exits |
| Unit | exits |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | The rotator keeps trying a rate-limited endpoint through more exits before pausing, which wastes more bytes when Roblox is limiting the endpoint itself rather than one IP. |
| If lowered | One or two burned exits pause the endpoint on the rotator sooner. At 1, any single 429 pauses it, which can idle a mostly healthy pool. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `egress#rotator` |
| Related settings | `rotator_cooldown_window_s`, `rotator_cooldown_s`, `cooldown_default_s` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | The rotator circuit breaker counts failures with the same distinct exit rule (plan 7.10). |

### `rotator_cooldown_window_s`

The time window in which the 429s from different exits, counted by rotator_cooldown_distinct_exits, must arrive to pause an endpoint on the rotator.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Rate-limited exits window |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 60 seconds (1m) |
| Range | 10 to 3600 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | 429s spread over a longer time still add up to a pause, so slow but steady rate limiting is caught and the endpoint is paused more often. |
| If lowered | Only 429s close together count, so the rotator keeps trying an endpoint that is rate-limited at a slow, steady pace, wasting more bytes. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |

### `rotator_hard_stop_pct`

When rotator usage in the current billing cycle reaches this share of rotator_quota_gb_per_month, Roxy stops using the rotator until the next cycle and sends anonymous traffic direct only. 0 means never stop. It only works when the quota is set.

| Type | percent |
| Default | 100 percent of quota |
| Range | 0 to 200 percent of quota |

| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Rotator use continues further into the quota; above 100 it continues past the plan's allowance, which may mean overage charges or a provider cutoff. |
| If lowered | The rotator stops earlier in the cycle, keeping some quota unused but losing the spillover path for the rest of the cycle sooner. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |

### `strict_host_allowlist`

When on, Roxy forwards requests only to the hosts listed in allowed_roblox_hosts. When off, any host under roblox.com is accepted. Limiting hosts is Roxy's main defense against server-side request forgery (SSRF: tricking a server into calling places it should not).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Strict Roblox host allowlist |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 1 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Requests for an unlisted subdomain get 404 "Not a Roblox URL", and the HOST-ADD recommendation proposes adding a host that real callers keep requesting. |
| If disabled | Any roblox.com subdomain is reachable through Roxy, including obscure or internal services you never meant to expose, which widens the SSRF attack surface. |
| Risk | high |
| High risk when | value = 0: Every roblox.com subdomain becomes reachable through Roxy, a much wider attack surface for server-side request forgery. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#hosts` |
| Related settings | `allowed_roblox_hosts` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Whatever this switch says, Roxy always refuses hosts outside roblox.com, raw IP addresses, ports other than 443 and URLs with a user name in them (plan 9.10). |

### `allowed_roblox_hosts`

The Roblox service hosts callers may reach through Roxy, written as full host names such as games.roblox.com. It is enforced while strict_host_allowlist is on. Every entry must be a roblox.com subdomain; other domains are always refused.

| Field | Value |

| Type | list[str] |
| Default | games.roblox.com, users.roblox.com, thumbnails.roblox.com, groups.roblox.com, catalog.roblox.com, economy.roblox.com, badges.roblox.com, presence.roblox.com, friends.roblox.com, inventory.roblox.com, avatar.roblox.com, apis.roblox.com, develop.roblox.com, accountinformation.roblox.com, accountsettings.roblox.com, premiumfeatures.roblox.com, followings.roblox.com, translations.roblox.com, locale.roblox.com, gamejoin.roblox.com, trades.roblox.com, notifications.roblox.com, points.roblox.com, billing.roblox.com, itemconfiguration.roblox.com, contacts.roblox.com, privatemessages.roblox.com, clientsettings.roblox.com, assetdelivery.roblox.com, auth.roblox.com, search.roblox.com, engagementpayouts.roblox.com, voice.roblox.com |
| Range | any |
| Unit | hosts |
| Step | none |
| If raised | Adding hosts makes more Roblox services reachable. Each added host gives attackers a little more room for server-side request forgery (SSRF), so add only hosts that real callers need. |
| If lowered | Callers of a removed host get 404 "Not a Roblox URL". |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | none |

| Related settings | `strict_host_allowlist`, `credential_probe_url` |

### `upstream_max_attempts`

The total number of upstream calls Roxy may make for one caller request when Roblox answers with a 5xx server error, times out, or the connection fails. Each retry waits a short random backoff, may use the other anonymous path, and only happens if the request deadline allows. A 429 (too many requests) or any other 4xx answer (the request itself was refused) is never retried this way.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Upstream attempts per request |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | int |
| Default | 2 attempts |
| Range | 1 to 5 attempts |
| Unit | attempts |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | More resilience against brief Roblox errors, but each failing request can cost Roblox more calls, adding load exactly when Roblox is struggling. |
| If lowered | Fewer upstream calls. At 1 there are no retries, so every brief Roblox error reaches the caller (as a 5xx with Retry-After, or as a stale cached copy when one exists). |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value >= 4: Each failing caller request can cost Roblox 4 or 5 calls during an outage; this kind of retry amplification helped get v1 rate-limited (plan 2.5, R3). |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#routing` |
| Related settings | `request_timeout`, `fallback_on_429`, `request_deadline_s`, `backoff_base_ms`, `backoff_cap_ms` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `UP-429-AMPLIFY`, `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | 1 to 3 |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | `max_retries_per_request` |
| v1 default | 3 attempts |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Wires up v1's max_retries_per_request, which existed but was never read (v1 always allowed up to 3 method picks through the constant MAX_METHOD_ATTEMPTS). The v1 value is never imported because its meaning changed (plan 18.3). |

### `fallback_on_429`

When Roblox answers a call with 429 (too many requests), allow exactly one retry through the other anonymous path (direct or rotator) if that path is healthy. A 429 is never retried onto the credential path.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Retry elsewhere after a 429 |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 0 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Some 429s are recovered by trying another IP, but each recovered request costs Roblox a second call while it is already limiting Roxy, which can make the limiting last longer. |
| If disabled | Strict backoff: a 429 opens a cooldown and the caller gets a stale cached copy or a 429 with Retry-After. Roblox is not called again for that endpoint and path until the cooldown ends. |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value = 1: Retrying 429s on another IP doubles calls while Roblox is already limiting Roxy; this cross-path retry was one of the causes of v1's repeated rate limiting (plan 2.5, R3). |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#routing` |
| Related settings | `upstream_max_attempts`, `rotator_enabled`, `direct_enabled` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `UP-429-AMPLIFY`, `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 1 |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | v1 always retried a 429 on the other method with no setting; v2 makes it an explicit opt-in. |

### `request_timeout`

How long Roxy waits for Roblox to send its response on one upstream attempt before giving up on that attempt. The overall request deadline (request_deadline_s) still limits all attempts together.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Upstream read timeout |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 15 seconds |
| Range | 1 to 120 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Fewer timeouts on slow endpoints, but a stuck call holds the caller and a connection longer, and fewer retries fit inside the request deadline. |
| If lowered | Slow calls fail sooner and free their slots, but endpoints that are slow yet working may start timing out. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#routing` |

| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 15 seconds |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

| Field | Value |

### `direct_user_agent`

The User-Agent header (the client identity text) Roxy sends to Roblox on the direct and credential paths. A caller's own User-Agent is never forwarded. The default is a desktop browser identity paired with API-style request headers (decision D23).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Upstream User-Agent |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | string |
| Default | "Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/141.0.0.0 Safari/537.36" |
| Range | any |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value = : An empty User-Agent makes upstream calls look like an unidentified script, which rate limiters often treat more harshly. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#routing` |
| Related settings | `ua_experiment_enabled`, `ua_experiment_alt_user_agent` |

| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | 200 |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Prefer changing it through the User-Agent experiment rather than by hand: a different identity can change how strictly Roblox rate-limits the server IP. The honest Roxy/2 identity becomes the default only after the experiment shows it is not limited more. Rotator sessions use their own consistent header profiles, not this value. |

### `ua_experiment_enabled`

Runs a side-by-side (A/B) test on direct traffic: calls are split between direct_user_agent and ua_experiment_alt_user_agent by a hash of the request's cache key for ua_experiment_days, and the UP-UA-EXPERIMENT recommendation compares the Roblox 429 rate of each identity.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | User-Agent experiment |
| Group | Routing and egress |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 0 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Part of direct traffic uses the alternative identity. If Roblox limits that identity more, 429s rise on that part during the experiment; the result comes with a confidence interval and offers the better identity as the new direct_user_agent. |
| If disabled | Every direct call uses direct_user_agent and no comparison data is collected. |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#routing` |
| Related settings | `ua_experiment_days`, `ua_experiment_alt_user_agent`, `direct_user_agent` |

### `ua_experiment_days`

How many days the User-Agent experiment runs before UP-UA-EXPERIMENT reports its result.

| Field | Value |

| Label | User-Agent experiment length |

| Default | 7 days |
| Range | 1 to 30 days |
| Unit | days |
| Step | 1 |

<a id="group-credential"></a>

## Credential

### `credential_enabled`

Master switch for Roxy's single Roblox account cookie (the credential). When on, Roxy may send it for its own liveness checks and for endpoints on the credential allowlist, which is empty by default, so public traffic never carries it. When off, no request Roxy makes to Roblox carries the account.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Use the Roblox credential |
| Group | Credential |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 1 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Scheduled liveness checks run every credential_probe_interval_min minutes and the Credential page shows whether the account still works. Requests to allowlisted endpoints (none by default) go out from the server's own IP with the account, paced by the credential rate limit. |
| If disabled | The account is never sent. Liveness checks and the credential health checks stop, so an expired or rejected cookie is not noticed until this is turned back on. Requests to allowlisted endpoints go out without the account, so endpoints that need a logged-in account fail for callers. The stored credential is kept; turning this off does not delete or replace it. |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `credential#status` |
| Related settings | `credential_probe_interval_min`, `credential_bucket_per_min` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | This is the quick, reversible way to stop all account use, for example while you investigate a leak guard alert. Replacing or deleting the credential itself is a separate, audited action on the Credential page, and Roxy never switches to another account on its own. |

### `credential_bucket_per_min`

The steady pace at which Roxy may send requests that carry the Roblox account, shared by all workers. Requests are spaced evenly (at 20 per minute, one every 3 seconds) instead of being allowed in clumps, and every credential call counts: allowlisted traffic, liveness checks, health checks and admin lookups.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Credential rate limit |
| Group | Credential |
| Type | int |
| Default | 20 requests per minute |
| Range | 1 to 120 requests per minute |
| Unit | requests per minute |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Allowlisted endpoints wait less, but the account makes more calls per minute from one server IP, which raises the chance of Roblox 429 answers on the account and of Roblox treating it as automated. v1 allowed about 88 per minute, and that account traffic was the largest cause of its Roblox 429s. |
| If lowered | The account is quieter and safer. Allowlisted requests wait longer for a turn, and when the wait runs out callers get a 429 with Retry-After instead of an answer. The value must stay above credential_probe_reserved_per_min so Roxy's own checks keep their reserved share. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value > 60: More than one account call per second on average. v1 was tuned to stay under about 100 calls per minute, and many Roblox endpoints allow one account far less, so at this pace 429s on the account, or a Roblox review of it, become likely. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `credential#budget` |
| Related settings | `credential_bucket_burst`, `credential_probe_reserved_per_min`, `global_bucket_per_min` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `UP-429-CREDENTIAL`, `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | `token_budget_requests` |
| v1 default | 95 requests per minute |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Replaces v1's token_budget_requests (95) per token_budget_window (65 seconds): about 88 calls per minute with no pacing, so the whole budget could leave in about one second. The meaning changed from a count per window to an evenly paced rate, so the v1 value is never imported. Credential calls also pass the global, host and endpoint buckets on Upstream > Buckets. |

### `credential_bucket_burst`

How many credential requests may go out back to back before the even pacing of credential_bucket_per_min applies. It lets a short cluster of calls through without waiting while the average stays at the configured rate.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Credential burst |
| Group | Credential |
| Type | int |
| Default | 3 requests |
| Range | 1 to 20 requests |
| Unit | requests |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Larger clusters of account calls leave at the same moment. Back-to-back calls from one account and one IP are the burst shape Roblox's rate limiters punish most, so 429s on the account become more likely. |
| If lowered | Account calls are spread out more smoothly. At 1, every credential call waits its full turn (3 seconds apart at the default rate), so allowlisted callers may wait longer. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value > 10: More than 10 account calls can leave in the same instant. Unpaced bursts on the account were a main cause of v1's Roblox 429s. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `credential#budget` |
| Related settings | `credential_bucket_per_min`, `credential_probe_reserved_per_min` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | v1 had no burst limit at all: its 95 call budget could be spent in about one second by its worker threads. The v1 budget window (token_budget_window, 65 seconds) is folded into credential_bucket_per_min, so it has no v2 key of its own. |

### `credential_probe_interval_min`

How often Roxy checks, on a schedule, that the credential still works by calling credential_probe_url with it. Each check is one account call, taken from the reserved probe share of the credential rate limit. 0 turns the scheduled check off; an admin can still run it by hand.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Credential check interval |
| Group | Credential |
| Type | int |
| Default | 30 minutes |
| Range | 0 to 1440 minutes |
| Unit | minutes |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Fewer account calls (48 per day at 30 minutes, 24 at 60), but an expired or rejected cookie is noticed later, so allowlisted endpoints may fail for longer before you are alerted. |
| If lowered | Expiry is noticed sooner, at the cost of more account calls: every 5 minutes is 288 calls per day just to check. More than 6 internal credential calls in an hour triggers the CRED-PROBE-COST recommendation. At 0 no scheduled check runs and expiry shows only when someone checks by hand. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | value is one of (1, 2, 3, 4): Checking more often than every 5 minutes spends more than 288 account calls per day on checks alone: a steady, machine-like pattern on the account that buys very little earlier warning. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `credential#status` |
| Related settings | `credential_probe_url`, `credential_probe_reserved_per_min`, `credential_enabled`, `health_auto_include_credential`, `insight_cred_probe_cost_max_per_hour` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `CRED-PROBE-COST`, `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Scheduled health runs call Roblox with the account only when health_auto_include_credential is 1. With credential_enabled at 0 no check runs, whatever this value is. Expected account use at the defaults with an empty allowlist is about 50 to 60 calls per day. |

### `credential_probe_url`

The Roblox address Roxy calls with the credential to check that it still works. It must be an https GET endpoint on an allowed Roblox host that returns the logged-in account's user id, so the check (H-CRED-AUTH) can also confirm the cookie still belongs to the same account.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Credential check URL |
| Group | Credential |
| Type | string |
| Default | "https://users.roblox.com/v1/users/authenticated" |
| Range | any |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value is not https://users.roblox.com/v1/users/authenticated: A different URL changes where the account cookie is sent on every check. If the new endpoint does not return the account's user id, Roxy can no longer confirm it is still the same account, and an endpoint with side effects could act on the account every time it is checked. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `credential#status` |
| Related settings | `credential_probe_interval_min`, `credential_enabled`, `allowed_roblox_hosts`, `strict_host_allowlist` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | "https://accountinformation.roblox.com/v1/birthdate" |
| Max length | 300 |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Was the fixed v1 constant TOKEN_PROBE_URL (accountinformation.roblox.com/v1/birthdate), which showed the cookie worked but not whose it was. The credential is only ever sent over https to allowed Roblox hosts, and redirects off roblox.com are never followed with it. |

### `credential_probe_reserved_per_min`

The part of credential_bucket_per_min kept for Roxy's own credential checks (scheduled liveness checks and health checks), in a separate sub-bucket. A check never waits behind allowlisted traffic, and allowlisted traffic can never use up the checks' share; it gets the rest of the rate.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Reserved rate for credential checks |
| Group | Credential |
| Type | int |
| Default | 2 requests per minute |
| Range | 1 to 10 requests per minute |
| Unit | requests per minute |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | More checks can run in the same minute without waiting, but less of the credential rate is left for allowlisted endpoints (at 20 per minute with a reserve of 5, only 15 remain). |
| If lowered | More of the credential rate goes to allowlisted traffic. When several checks start together, for example a health run during a scheduled check, they wait behind each other, which only delays the result. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `credential#budget` |
| Related settings | `credential_bucket_per_min`, `credential_probe_interval_min` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Checked when saved: the reserve must be strictly smaller than credential_bucket_per_min. |

### `credential_cooldown_default_s`

How long Roxy stops using the credential, on every worker, after Roblox answers a credential request with 429 (too many requests) and no Retry-After header. When Roblox does say how long to wait, Roxy waits that long instead. During the rest, allowlisted endpoints answer 503 with Retry-After.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Credential rest after a 429 |
| Group | Credential |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 60 seconds (1m) |
| Range | 5 to 3600 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | The account rests longer after a 429, which makes a repeat 429 less likely, but allowlisted endpoints are unavailable to callers for longer. |
| If lowered | The account is used again sooner after a 429. Coming back too early tends to earn another 429, and repeated 429s on one account are what can get it reviewed by Roblox. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value < 15: Below 15 seconds Roxy returns to the account almost as soon as Roblox asked it to slow down, the pattern most likely to turn one 429 on the account into many. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `credential#budget`, `upstream#cooldowns` |
| Related settings | `cooldown_default_s`, `cooldown_min_s`, `cooldown_max_s`, `credential_bucket_per_min` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | `token_expiration_cooldown` |
| v1 default | 15 seconds |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Replaces v1's token_expiration_cooldown (15 seconds), which paused only one worker and then spent an extra account call to re-check the cookie. In v2 the rest is shared by every worker and a 429 is never mistaken for an expired cookie. The meaning changed, so the v1 value is never imported. |

<a id="group-upstream"></a>

## Upstream pacing and resilience

### `global_bucket_per_min`

The ceiling on how many calls Roxy makes to Roblox per minute in total, across every egress path and every worker. Every upstream call counts, including retries, CSRF handshakes, background refreshes and health checks. When it is used up, calls wait in the queue, are answered from an older cached copy, or get a 429 with Retry-After.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Global upstream rate |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | int |
| Default | 600 requests per minute |
| Range | 1 to 10000 requests per minute |
| Unit | requests per minute |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | More total throughput before queuing starts, but more calls reach Roblox, so the server IP and the rotator exits are more likely to be rate limited (429) on many endpoints at once. |
| If lowered | Fewer calls reach Roblox and 429s are rarer, but more requests wait, more answers come from older cached copies, and callers get more 429 'busy' answers when no cached copy exists. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value > 1200: More than 20 calls per second to Roblox in total, twice the default. The egress, host and endpoint buckets still apply, but this ceiling stops being a safety net against a flood of cache misses. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#buckets` |
| Related settings | `global_bucket_burst`, `direct_bucket_per_min`, `rotator_bucket_per_min`, `credential_bucket_per_min`, `queue_wait_interactive_ms` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `UP-BUCKET-TUNE`, `UP-QUEUE-SAT`, `UP-LATENCY`, `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Background cache refreshes only take global slots while this bucket is less than half reserved, so they never take a slot a caller could use soon. |

### `global_bucket_burst`

How many upstream calls, in total, may leave back to back before the steady pace of global_bucket_per_min applies. A small burst absorbs short spikes without making callers wait.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Global upstream burst |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | int |
| Default | 30 requests |
| Range | 1 to 500 requests |
| Unit | requests |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Larger spikes go straight to Roblox without waiting; sudden clumps of calls are the shape Roblox's rate limiters punish most. |
| If lowered | Spikes are smoothed into an even stream, so more calls wait briefly in the queue during a spike. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | value > 100: More than 100 calls may hit Roblox in the same instant. Unpaced bursts were a main cause of v1's Roblox 429s. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#buckets` |
| Related settings | `global_bucket_per_min` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `UP-BUCKET-TUNE`, `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

### `direct_bucket_per_min`

How many anonymous calls per minute Roxy may send to Roblox from the server's own IP address (the direct path), across all workers. The direct path carries all public traffic by default, so this is usually the bucket that fills first.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Direct path rate |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | int |
| Default | 300 requests per minute |
| Range | 1 to 10000 requests per minute |
| Unit | requests per minute |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | More public traffic is served from the server IP before Roxy queues it or shifts it to the rotator. Roblox limits each IP, so the server IP is more likely to get 429s. |
| If lowered | The server IP is gentler on Roblox and less likely to be rate limited, but more requests wait, are answered from older cached copies, or spill to the rotator (billed per byte) when it is enabled and within budget. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value > 600: More than 10 anonymous calls per second from one IP address. Roblox limits traffic per IP, so the server IP is likely to be rate limited on many endpoints, which hurts every caller. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#buckets` |
| Related settings | `direct_bucket_burst`, `global_bucket_per_min`, `direct_shift_threshold_pct`, `direct_weight`, `rotator_bucket_per_min` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `UP-BUCKET-TUNE`, `UP-QUEUE-SAT`, `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | When this bucket is fuller than direct_shift_threshold_pct, routing shifts weight toward the rotator, but only if the rotator is enabled and within its byte budget. |

How many direct path calls may leave the server IP back to back before the steady pace of direct_bucket_per_min applies.

### `rotator_bucket_per_min`

How many calls per minute Roxy may send through the rotating proxy service (DataImpulse), whose exits use many different IP addresses, across all workers. The rotator only ever carries anonymous traffic, never the credential, and every byte through it is billed.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Rotator rate |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | int |
| Default | 300 requests per minute |
| Range | 1 to 10000 requests per minute |
| Unit | requests per minute |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | The rotator can take more load when the direct path is busy or cooling down, at the cost of more billed bytes and faster use of the monthly quota. |
| If lowered | Less rotator spending and quota use. When the direct path is also busy, more requests wait, are answered from older cached copies, or get 429 'busy' answers. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |

| Item range | none |

| Notes | With the default rotator_weight of 0 the rotator is used only when the direct path cannot serve a request in time or an endpoint rule prefers it, so this limit rarely binds. The monthly byte quota and the daily cap apply on top of it. |

The rate each Roblox service host (for example games.roblox.com or users.roblox.com) gets unless it has its own limit on Upstream > Buckets. Roblox runs each service separately, so this keeps one busy service from being hammered even when the overall rate is fine.

### `adaptive_rate_enabled`

Lets Roxy tune each endpoint's rate by itself: after a Roblox 429 it lowers that endpoint's rate, and after a long clean period with real demand above the limit it raises it a little. Every change is written as a per-endpoint limit you can see and edit on Upstream > Buckets.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Adaptive per-endpoint rate |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 1 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | On a Roblox 429 the endpoint's rate drops by adaptive_decrease_pct (never below adaptive_min_per_min); after adaptive_probe_after_h clean hours with demand above the limit it rises by adaptive_increase_pct (never above adaptive_max_per_min). When several endpoints of one host get 429s together, the host's rate is lowered instead. |
| If disabled | Rates stay exactly where they are and change only when an admin edits them or applies a recommendation. Cooldowns after a 429 still happen; only the automatic rate tuning stops, so repeated 429s on one endpoint are more likely. |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#buckets` |
| Related settings | `adaptive_decrease_pct`, `adaptive_increase_pct`, `adaptive_probe_after_h`, `adaptive_min_per_min`, `adaptive_max_per_min`, `endpoint_bucket_default_per_min` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `UP-BUCKET-TUNE` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Every adjustment is logged with its evidence and shown in UP-BUCKET-TUNE recommendations. |

### `adaptive_decrease_pct`

How much the adaptive controller cuts an endpoint's rate each time Roblox answers it with 429. At 30, an endpoint at 120 per minute drops to 84.

| Default | 30 percent |
| Range | 5 to 90 percent |
| Unit | percent |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Roxy backs off harder after a 429, so a repeat 429 is less likely, but the endpoint queues more and serves older cached copies until its rate climbs back, which is slow (see adaptive_increase_pct). |
| If lowered | Gentler cuts keep more throughput after a 429, but Roxy may need several 429s in a row to find a rate Roblox accepts. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |

### `aimd_enabled`

An optional extra limit on how many calls may be in flight at the same time to each Roblox host on each egress path. It grows the limit slowly while calls succeed and cuts it sharply on a 429, a timeout or a server error. Off by default, because Roblox limits request rate rather than parallel calls, and the rate buckets already handle rate.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Adaptive concurrency (AIMD) |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 0 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Each host and egress pair starts at aimd_initial parallel calls, gains 1 after every aimd_increase_after successes in a row up to aimd_max, and is multiplied by aimd_decrease_factor on trouble (never below aimd_min). Calls beyond the limit wait in the queue, and the Upstream page charts the limit against calls in flight. |
| If disabled | Only the rate buckets pace upstream calls; the number of calls in flight is bounded only by the connection pools. |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#concurrency` |
| Related settings | `aimd_initial`, `aimd_min`, `aimd_max`, `aimd_increase_after`, `aimd_decrease_factor` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Useful only for a host whose response time collapses under parallel load. AIMD stands for additive increase, multiplicative decrease, the same idea TCP uses to avoid network congestion. In-flight slots expire on their own, so a crashed worker cannot leak them. |

### `aimd_initial`

| Default | 8 concurrent requests |
| Range | 1 to 256 concurrent requests |
| Unit | concurrent requests |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | More parallel calls at the start, before Roxy has learned how much each host tolerates. |
| If lowered | A cautious start; parallelism grows as calls succeed. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |

### `cooldown_default_s`

How long Roxy stops calling an endpoint through one egress path, on every worker, after Roblox answers 429 without a Retry-After header. Repeated 429s on the same endpoint double it (30, 60, 120 seconds and so on, with a little randomness) up to cooldown_max_s. During a cooldown callers get an older cached copy when one exists, otherwise a 429 with Retry-After.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Default cooldown after a 429 |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 30 seconds |
| Range | 1 to 3600 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Roxy rests a limited endpoint longer, which makes a repeat 429 less likely; callers see older cached data or 429 answers for longer. |
| If lowered | Roxy returns to a limited endpoint sooner. If Roblox's limit has not reset yet, the next call earns another 429 and a longer cooldown. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value < 5: Coming back within seconds of a 429 with no Retry-After nearly always earns another 429, and turns a short limit into a long one for every caller. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#cooldowns` |
| Related settings | `cooldown_min_s`, `cooldown_max_s`, `credential_cooldown_default_s`, `cooldown_host_escalation_endpoints` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | When Roblox does send Retry-After, or an x-ratelimit-remaining of 0 with a reset time, Roxy waits as long as Roblox asks instead, kept between cooldown_min_s and cooldown_max_s. A credential 429 uses credential_cooldown_default_s instead of this value. |

### `cooldown_min_s`

The shortest cooldown Roxy will use after a 429, even when Roblox's Retry-After header asks for less (or for zero).

| Field | Value |

| Label | Shortest cooldown |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 1 seconds |
| Range | 1 to 3600 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Roxy rests endpoints longer than Roblox asked, which is safer for the server IP but serves older cached data or 429 answers for longer than necessary. |
| If lowered | Roxy follows short Retry-After values closely; 1 second is the most faithful setting. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |

| Notes | Checked when saved: must be no higher than cooldown_max_s. |

### `cooldown_max_s`

The longest cooldown Roxy will use after a 429. It caps both Roblox's Retry-After value and the doubling of cooldown_default_s for repeated 429s, so one odd header cannot park an endpoint for hours.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Longest cooldown |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 600 seconds (10m) |
| Range | 1 to 3600 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Roxy honors longer Retry-After values and lets repeated-429 backoff grow further, so persistent limits are respected; an endpoint may be answered only from cache or with 429s for longer. |
| If lowered | Endpoints come back sooner, but Roxy may cut a longer Roblox Retry-After short and call again before Roblox said it could, which usually earns another 429. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value < 60: Any Retry-After longer than this is cut short, so Roxy calls Roblox again before Roblox said it could. Ignoring Retry-After is what turned v1's 429s into repeated blocks. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#cooldowns` |
| Related settings | `cooldown_min_s`, `cooldown_default_s` |

| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |

| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |

| Notes | Checked when saved: must be no lower than cooldown_min_s. |

### `cooldown_host_escalation_endpoints`

How many different endpoints of one Roblox host must get a 429 within cooldown_host_escalation_window_s before Roxy cools down the whole host on that egress path, instead of each endpoint alone. Several endpoints failing together usually means Roblox is limiting the host or the IP, not one API.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Endpoints that escalate to a host cooldown |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | int |
| Default | 3 endpoint templates |
| Range | 2 to 50 endpoint templates |
| Unit | endpoint templates |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Host-wide cooldowns are rarer, so the host's other endpoints keep flowing, but Roxy may keep calling a host that is limiting it as a whole. |
| If lowered | Host-wide cooldowns happen sooner, which is safer for the server IP but answers more of the host's healthy endpoints from cache or with 429s. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#cooldowns` |
| Related settings | `cooldown_host_escalation_window_s`, `cooldown_default_s`, `host_bucket_default_per_min` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `UP-429-HOST` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Rotator 429s follow their own rule (rotator_cooldown_distinct_exits), because each rotator session is a different exit IP. |

### `cooldown_host_escalation_window_s`

The time window in which cooldown_host_escalation_endpoints different endpoints of one host must get a 429 for the cooldown to cover the whole host.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Host escalation window |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 60 seconds (1m) |
| Range | 10 to 3600 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | 429s spread further apart still count together, so host-wide cooldowns happen more often (safer, more answers from cache). |
| If lowered | Only 429s close together count, so host-wide cooldowns are rarer and the host's other endpoints keep flowing. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |

| High risk when | none |

### `breaker_failure_threshold`

A circuit breaker stops Roxy from calling an endpoint (or a whole host) on one egress path while it is failing, instead of piling on. It opens when at least this many calls fail within breaker_window_s and the failed share is above breaker_failure_ratio; failures are timeouts, connection errors and Roblox server errors (5xx), and a 429 opens it at once for the Retry-After time.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Breaker failure count |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | int |
| Default | 5 failures |
| Range | 1 to 1000 failures |
| Unit | failures |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Breakers open less readily, so Roxy keeps calling a failing endpoint longer and callers wait for errors instead of getting a quick answer from cache. |
| If lowered | Breakers open after fewer failures, protecting callers and Roblox sooner, but a few unlucky errors on a quiet endpoint can trip one. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#breakers` |
| Related settings | `breaker_window_s`, `breaker_failure_ratio`, `breaker_open_s` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `UP-5XX`, `UP-BREAKER-FLAP` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

### `breaker_open_s`

How long an open breaker blocks calls before letting a single test call through. If that call succeeds the breaker closes; if it fails, the breaker reopens for twice as long, up to 600 seconds. While it is open, callers get an older cached copy when one exists, otherwise a 429 with Retry-After.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Breaker open time |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 30 seconds |
| Range | 1 to 600 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | A failing endpoint is left alone longer, which helps it recover, but callers keep getting cached data or errors for a while after it is healthy again. |
| If lowered | Roxy tests a failing endpoint sooner and recovers faster, but may keep poking one that is still down. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#breakers` |
| Related settings | `breaker_failure_threshold`, `breaker_window_s`, `breaker_failure_ratio` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `UP-BREAKER-FLAP` |
| Auto-apply bounds | 15 to 120 |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Only one test call runs at a time across all workers, so a recovering endpoint is not stampeded. |

### `backoff_base_ms`

Before Roxy retries a call that failed with a Roblox server error (5xx), a timeout or a connection error, it waits a random time that starts around this value and grows with each retry up to backoff_cap_ms. The randomness (jitter) keeps workers from retrying in lockstep; 429s are never retried this way.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Retry wait, starting value |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 200 ms |
| Range | 10 to 10000 ms |
| Unit | ms |
| Step | 10 |
| If raised | Retries wait longer, giving Roblox more time to recover; each failing request takes longer to finish. |
| If lowered | Retries come faster, so a briefly failing Roblox service is hit again sooner. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#cooldowns`, `upstream#retries` |
| Related settings | `backoff_cap_ms`, `upstream_max_attempts`, `request_timeout` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Checked when saved: must be no higher than backoff_cap_ms. |

| Unit | ms |
| Step | 10 |
| If raised | Retries can spread out more; a request that needs retries may take longer overall. |
| If lowered | Retries stay close together, so failing requests finish sooner. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |

### `queue_wait_interactive_ms`

When the buckets have no free slot, how long a caller's request may wait for one if Roxy has no cached copy to fall back on. After that the caller gets a 429 with a Retry-After telling it when to come back.

| Field | Value |

| Range | 0 to 20000 ms |
| Unit | ms |
| Step | 100 |
| If raised | Fewer 429 'busy' answers to callers, but they wait longer, and each waiting request holds a connection open. |

### `queue_max_length`

The most requests one worker process may hold waiting for an upstream slot. When it is full the lowest priority waiter is dropped: background refreshes first, then callers, who get an older cached copy or a 429 with Retry-After. With 2 workers the whole server can hold twice this many.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Queue length per worker |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | int |
| Default | 500 requests |
| Range | 10 to 10000 requests |
| Unit | requests |
| Step | 10 |
| If raised | Fewer drops during spikes, but more open connections and memory held by waiting requests. |
| If lowered | Less memory held during spikes; more requests are dropped early with a 'busy' answer. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | value > 2000: This server has less than 1 GB of memory. Thousands of waiting requests per worker, each holding a connection and its request body, can push a worker past its memory limit, and the system then restarts it, dropping every request it held. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#queue` |
| Related settings | `queue_wait_interactive_ms`, `queue_wait_background_ms` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `UP-QUEUE-SAT`, `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Fairness between workers does not come from this cap but from each priority's maximum wait: a caller may reserve a slot further ahead than a background refresh can. |

### `request_deadline_s`

The longest any single proxy request may take inside Roxy, from arrival to answer, including queue waits, retries and backoff. When it runs out the caller gets a 504 with Retry-After. Every inner time budget is derived from it, so they can never add up past it.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Request deadline |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 60 seconds (1m) |
| Range | 10 to 90 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Slow requests may finish instead of ending in a 504, but each one holds a connection and worker resources longer. |
| If lowered | Stuck requests end sooner with a 504; slow but valid upstream calls with retries may be cut off. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#queue` |
| Related settings | `queue_wait_interactive_ms`, `backoff_cap_ms`, `request_timeout`, `upstream_max_attempts`, `tarpit_max_seconds` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Saving is refused if queue_wait_interactive_ms plus request_timeout times upstream_max_attempts plus backoff_cap_ms (36 seconds at the defaults), or tarpit_max_seconds, would exceed this value minus 2 seconds. The maximum of 90 keeps it at least 10 seconds below nginx's 100 second proxy_read_timeout, so Roxy always answers before nginx gives up. |

### `csrf_token_cache_s`

Roblox requires an x-csrf-token header on write requests (POST, PATCH, PUT, DELETE): a write without a valid one gets a 403 carrying a fresh token, and Roxy retries once with it. This is how long Roxy keeps reusing that token, so later writes skip the extra 403 round trip. 0 turns reuse off.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Roblox CSRF token reuse |
| Group | Upstream pacing and resilience |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 600 seconds (10m) |
| Range | 0 to 3600 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Fewer extra 403 round trips on write requests, but a token Roblox has already retired is reused longer, which shows up as repeated 403s (see the UP-CSRF-LOOP recommendation). |
| If lowered | Tokens are refreshed more often: more 403 handshakes, each one an extra upstream call that counts against the buckets, but fewer failures from old tokens. At 0 every write request first gets a 403. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `upstream#retries` |
| Related settings | `global_bucket_per_min` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `UP-CSRF-LOOP` |
| Auto-apply bounds | 60 to 600 |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Tokens are kept separately for each egress identity (the server IP, the credential, or one rotator session) and are never sent to callers. The single CSRF retry always uses the same egress path. |

<a id="group-cache"></a>

## Cache

### `cache_enabled`

Master switch for the response cache. When on, Roxy answers a repeat request for the same Roblox URL from a stored copy instead of calling Roblox again. This is the strongest protection against Roblox rate limits (429 responses), because it works no matter how many different callers ask.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Response cache |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 1 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Repeat requests are answered from the cache according to the lifetime settings and the cache rules, so Roblox sees about one call per distinct request per lifetime instead of one per caller. |
| If disabled | Every caller request goes to Roblox. Upstream calls, Roblox 429s and response times rise sharply, and no stale copy exists to hide Roblox errors or cooldowns from callers. Use only briefly, for debugging. |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value = 0: Every request goes to Roblox, the traffic pattern that got v1 rate-limited 579 times in under 50,000 requests. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings` |
| Related settings | `cache_disk_enabled`, `cache_ttl_seconds`, `cache_stale_seconds`, `cache_coalesce` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `CACHE-OFF`, `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 1 |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Migration from v1 imports this value only when it differs from the v1 default (1). |

### `cache_ttl_seconds`

How long a successful (2xx) Roblox response stays fresh in the cache when no cache rule matches its endpoint (this lifetime is often called the TTL, time to live). Cache rules on the Cache page override it per endpoint, including a rule of 0 that turns caching off for one endpoint.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Default cache lifetime |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 120 seconds (2m) |
| Range | 0 to 86400 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Fewer upstream calls and fewer Roblox 429s for endpoints without a rule, but callers may see data up to this many seconds old (plus the stale-while-revalidate window). |
| If lowered | Callers see fresher data, at the cost of more upstream calls and more 429 risk. 0 stops caching every endpoint that has no cache rule of its own. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | value = 0: Endpoints without their own cache rule are never cached, so every repeat request for them goes to Roblox.<br>value > 3600: Endpoints without their own cache rule can serve data more than an hour old, which breaks callers that expect live values such as player counts or presence. Give long lifetimes to specific endpoints with cache rules instead. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings` |
| Related settings | `cache_swr_seconds`, `cache_stale_seconds`, `cache_error_ttl_seconds`, `cache_default_rules_enabled`, `ttl_tuner_enabled` |
| Related rules | `cache_rules` |
| Related recommendations | `CACHE-TTL-TUNE`, `CACHE-LOW-HIT`, `HOT-ENDPOINT`, `UP-429-ENDPOINT`, `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
