### `cache_error_ttl_seconds`

How long Roxy remembers a Roblox error answer of 400, 403, 404 or 410 for the same request and replays it, with the original status, instead of asking Roblox again. A 403 is cached only when it means permission denied (not a CSRF security-token challenge), and 429 and 5xx answers are never cached as content.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Error answer lifetime |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 60 seconds (1m) |
| Range | 0 to 3600 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Callers that keep asking for something that does not exist cost fewer upstream calls; a resource that starts to exist (for example a newly created item) is noticed later. |
| If lowered | Errors are rechecked with Roblox sooner, so repeated bad lookups cost more upstream calls and more 429 risk. 0 sends every repeat of a failing request to Roblox. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings` |
| Related settings | `cache_ttl_seconds`, `cache_negative_429` |
| Related rules | `cache_rules` |
| Related recommendations | `CACHE-NEG`, `UP-4XX-SPIKE` |
| Auto-apply bounds | 30 to 600 |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 0 seconds |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | v1 did not cache errors by default (0). Migration imports a v1 value only when it differs from 0. Cache rules can set their own error lifetime (negative TTL) per endpoint. |

### `cache_swr_seconds`

For this many seconds after an entry expires, Roxy still serves it instantly (header Roxy-Cache: REVALIDATING) and starts one background refresh, so the next caller gets new data and nobody waits. This is called stale-while-revalidate; cache rules can set their own window.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Stale-while-revalidate window |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 60 seconds (1m) |
| Range | 0 to 86400 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | More callers get an instant answer that may be slightly old, and fewer callers wait on a fetch when a popular entry expires. |
| If lowered | Served data is fresher, but more callers wait for the refetch when an entry expires. 0 turns stale-while-revalidate off. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings` |
| Related settings | `cache_ttl_seconds`, `cache_stale_seconds`, `swr_max_inflight`, `queue_wait_background_ms` |
| Related rules | `cache_rules` |
| Related recommendations | `UP-429-ENDPOINT`, `UP-5XX`, `UP-LATENCY` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Each background refresh is one upstream call at low priority, shared by all workers (only one worker refreshes a given entry). |

### `cache_stale_seconds`

How long after expiry an entry may still be served when Roblox cannot be asked: the endpoint is cooling down after a 429, its circuit breaker is open (calls paused after repeated failures), or the upstream attempt failed. The caller gets the old copy (Roxy-Cache: STALE) instead of an error.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Stale serving window |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 600 seconds (10m) |
| Range | 0 to 86400 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Callers ride out longer Roblox outages and cooldowns with old data instead of errors, and the data they get can be older. Entries also stay in the shared cache longer, using more disk. |
| If lowered | Old data is never served past this limit, so callers see more 429 and 5xx errors during cooldowns and outages. 0 never serves a stale copy. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings` |
| Related settings | `cache_swr_seconds`, `cache_ttl_seconds`, `queue_wait_stale_ms`, `cooldown_default_s`, `breaker_open_s` |
| Related rules | `cache_rules` |
| Related recommendations | `UP-5XX` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 600 seconds (10m) |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Measured from the moment the entry expires, so keep it at least as long as cache_swr_seconds. Migration imports a v1 value only when it differs from the v1 default (600). |

### `cache_disk_enabled`

Use the shared cache tier stored in cache.db, an SQLite database on disk. Every worker process reads and writes it, so one fetch serves callers on all workers and cached data survives restarts and deploys.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Shared disk cache |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 1 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | One shared copy per request for the whole fleet, up to cache_max_entries and cache_max_bytes; each worker keeps a small memory tier in front of it for the hottest entries. |
| If disabled | Memory only: each worker keeps its own small cache (cache_memory_entries, cache_memory_bytes), so the same request is fetched once per worker, far fewer entries fit, and everything is lost on restart or deploy. Useful only while cache.db is failing. |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value = 0: The hit ratio drops sharply and upstream calls multiply by the number of workers, raising the risk of Roblox 429s. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings` |
| Related settings | `cache_max_entries`, `cache_max_bytes`, `cache_memory_entries`, `cache_memory_bytes` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |

| v1 default | 1 |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Migration from v1 imports this value only when it differs from the v1 default (1). |

### `cache_max_entries`

The most entries the shared cache tier (cache.db) may hold across all workers. When it is full, the eviction policy decides which entries are removed. 0 holds nothing in the shared tier.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Shared cache entry limit |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | int |
| Default | 200000 entries |
| Range | 0 to 5000000 entries |
| Unit | entries |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | More distinct requests stay cached, so fewer misses and upstream calls; the database and its index grow a little. |
| If lowered | More evictions, so more misses and upstream calls. 0 stores nothing on disk, the same as turning cache_disk_enabled off. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | value = 0: Nothing is stored in the shared tier, so each worker only has its small memory cache and upstream calls multiply. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings` |
| Related settings | `cache_max_bytes`, `cache_eviction_policy`, `cache_disk_enabled` |
| Related rules | none |

| Sensitive | no |

| Max length | none |

| Item range | none |

| Notes | v1 caps were sized for JSON shard files. Migration imports a v1 value only when it differs from the v1 default (3000) and is larger than this default; a smaller v1 value is reported, not imported. |

### `cache_max_bytes`

Total size budget of the shared cache tier in cache.db, counting bodies as stored on disk (compressed when cache_compress is on). When it is exceeded, the eviction policy removes entries until it fits. 0 holds nothing in the shared tier.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Shared cache size limit |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | bytes |
| Default | 536870912 bytes (512 MiB) |
| Range | 0 to 17179869184 bytes (at most 16 GiB) |
| Unit | bytes |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | More data stays cached and the hit ratio rises, using more disk (counted toward storage_total_budget_gb). |
| If lowered | Less disk, more evictions and misses. 0 stores nothing on disk. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | value = 0: Nothing is stored in the shared tier, so each worker only has its small memory cache and upstream calls multiply.<br>value > 4294967296: The cache alone would take more than a third of the default 12 GB storage budget; if the disk fills, every database write fails and Roxy degrades. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings` |

| Auto-apply bounds | 268435456 to 4294967296 |
| Sensitive | no |

| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |

### `cache_max_body`

The largest response body, in bytes of the uncompressed body, that may be cached. Bigger responses are passed to the caller but never stored, and never stored cut short, because half a JSON document is worse than no cache. 0 caches nothing.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Largest cacheable response |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | bytes |
| Default | 1048576 bytes (1 MiB) |
| Range | 0 to 8388608 bytes (at most 8 MiB) |
| Unit | bytes |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Large responses (big batch lookups, long lists) get cached too; each one uses more memory and disk, so fewer entries fit in the same budget. |
| If lowered | Large responses always go to Roblox, leaving more room for small entries. 0 caches nothing, so the cache is effectively off. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | value = 0: No response fits, so the cache is effectively off and every request goes to Roblox. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings` |
| Related settings | `cache_max_bytes`, `cache_memory_bytes` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 262144 bytes (256 KiB) |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | v1 counted characters and treated 0 as no limit; v2 counts bytes and keeps every store bounded, so 0 now caches nothing. Migration imports a v1 value only when it differs from the v1 default (256 KiB) and is larger than this default. |

### `cache_memory_entries`

The most entries each worker keeps in its own in-memory cache tier, a small fast layer in front of cache.db. It is per worker, so the fleet holds up to this many times the number of workers. 0 turns the memory tier off.

| Field | Value |

| Group | Cache |

| If raised | More hits are answered from RAM without a database read; this only helps while cache_memory_bytes still has room. |
| If lowered | More lookups read cache.db, which is still fast but slower than RAM. 0 sends every lookup to cache.db. |
| If enabled | n/a |

### `cache_memory_bytes`

RAM budget of each worker's in-memory cache tier. It is per worker, so total use is this times ROXY_WORKERS (2 by default), and it doubles during a deploy while both colors (old and new release) run. This is the cache setting that can run the server out of memory.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Memory cache size per worker |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | bytes |
| Default | 16777216 bytes (16 MiB) |
| Range | 0 to 1073741824 bytes (at most 1 GiB) |
| Unit | bytes |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | More hits are served from RAM, but each worker uses more memory and gets closer to the systemd memory cap (MemoryMax), past which the kernel kills and restarts workers. |
| If lowered | Less RAM and more reads from cache.db. This is the first knob to lower when memory is tight. 0 turns the memory tier off. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value > 33554432: With 2 workers and the default systemd cap of MemoryMax=420M per color, more than 32 MiB per worker can push a color over its cap, and the kernel then kills workers mid-request. |

| Max length | none |

| Item range | none |

| Notes | The default (16 MiB) is sized for a server with about 1 GB of RAM (systemd MemoryHigh=320M and MemoryMax=420M per color); the plan's 64 MiB suits a 2 GB server with a higher cap. Migration imports a v1 value only when it differs from the v1 default (8 MiB) and is larger than this default. |

### `cache_eviction_policy`

How the shared cache tier chooses which entries to remove when it is over cache_max_entries or cache_max_bytes. Entries whose stale window has ended are always removed first; this choice decides which still-useful entries go next.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Eviction policy |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | enum |
| Default | "hybrid" |
| Range | one of `lru`, `lfu`, `hybrid` |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings` |
| Related settings | `cache_max_entries`, `cache_max_bytes` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | v1 always removed the oldest stored entries first (first in, first out). |

Options:

- `lru` (Least recently used (LRU)): When the cache is over a limit, remove the entry that has gone longest without being served. Simple, and good when the set of popular requests keeps changing.
- `lfu` (Least frequently used (LFU)): Remove the entry with the fewest hits. Keeps long-term favorites, but an entry that was popular yesterday can linger after demand moves on.
- `hybrid` (Hybrid (LFU with recency decay)): Count hits but let old hits fade over time, so entries that are both popular and recent stay. Recommended for Roxy's mix of steady hot endpoints and short bursts.

### `cache_compress`

Store response bodies in cache.db compressed with zstd, a fast compression format. Roblox JSON usually shrinks several times over, so the same disk budget holds many more entries.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Compress cached bodies |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 1 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | New entries are stored compressed, so cache_max_bytes holds several times more of them; each store and each disk read costs a little CPU. |
| If disabled | New entries are stored uncompressed: more disk per entry, so the byte limit fills sooner, and slightly less CPU per store and read. Entries already stored compressed stay readable. |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings` |
| Related settings | `cache_max_bytes` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

### `cache_coalesce`

Fleet-wide request coalescing, also called single-flight. When many callers ask for the same uncached URL at once, one worker fetches it from Roblox and the others wait for that answer instead of each making its own call.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Request coalescing |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 1 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | A burst of requests for one missing or expired entry makes a single Roblox call, and the waiting callers get the shared answer (Roxy-Cache: COALESCED). |
| If disabled | Every concurrent caller of a missing entry makes its own Roblox call, so a popular endpoint expiring produces a burst of identical calls, the pattern that triggers Roblox 429s. |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value = 0: A cold or expired popular entry sends one Roblox call per waiting caller at the same moment, which is exactly the burst the cache exists to prevent. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings`, `cache#coalescing` |
| Related settings | `cache_coalesce_wait_ms`, `cache_enabled` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 1 |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | v1 coalesced only within one worker; v2 coalesces across all workers. Credential and anonymous fetches of the same URL are never coalesced together. |

### `cache_coalesce_wait_ms`

The longest a waiting caller (a follower) waits for the worker that is already fetching the same URL (the owner). 0 means wait as long as the owner is allowed to take, about 36 seconds with default settings (queue_wait_interactive_ms plus request_timeout times upstream_max_attempts plus backoff_cap_ms).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Coalescing wait limit |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 0 ms |
| Range | 0 to 60000 ms |
| Unit | ms |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Followers wait longer for the shared answer, so fewer of them give up; values above the owner's own deadline change nothing. |
| If lowered | Followers give up sooner and get a stale copy or a 503 with Retry-After (reason coalesce_timeout). They never call Roblox themselves, so this never adds upstream calls. Setting exactly 0 means the full owner deadline, not zero wait. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings`, `cache#coalescing` |
| Related settings | `cache_coalesce`, `queue_wait_interactive_ms`, `request_timeout`, `upstream_max_attempts`, `backoff_cap_ms`, `request_deadline_s` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 1500 ms (1s) |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | The meaning changed: in v1, 1500 ms was a short wait inside one worker. The v1 value is therefore never imported. |

### `cache_post_requests`

Which POST requests may be cached. Roblox uses POST for some read-only batch lookups that are safe and valuable to cache, while other POSTs are writes. A cached POST answer is keyed on a hash of the request body, so different lookups never share an answer.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | POST caching |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | enum |
| Default | "allowlist" |
| Range | one of `off`, `allowlist`, `all` |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value = all: Write requests can be answered from the cache, so a caller gets someone else's earlier result and the write never reaches Roblox. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings` |
| Related settings | `cache_ttl_seconds` |
| Related rules | `cache_rules` |
| Related recommendations | `UP-429-ENDPOINT`, `CACHE-LOW-HIT`, `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 0 |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | v1 had an on/off switch (default off). Migration maps v1 on to all, with a high-risk warning in its report, and does not import v1 off, so the allowlist default applies. |

Options:

- `off` (Off): Never cache POST responses. Every POST goes to Roblox, including read-only batch lookups such as users by id, which are among the most called endpoints.
- `allowlist` (Allowlisted lookups): Cache POST only for the built-in read-only batch lookups (users by id, usernames, thumbnails batch, presence, place details) and for cache rules that explicitly allow POST. Recommended.
- `all` (All POST requests (risky)): Cache every POST response. POST usually means a write (sending, buying, changing something), and caching one replays an old answer to a later caller instead of performing the action.

### `cache_respect_no_cache`

Whether a caller can skip the cache by sending the header Cache-Control: no-cache (or no-store). Off by default, because it would give anyone flooding Roxy a one-header way past the cache straight to Roblox.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Honor caller no-cache headers |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 0 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Any caller that sends the header bypasses the cache and makes a fresh Roblox call, adding upstream load and 429 risk. Use only when every caller is a trusted integration that needs forced refreshes. |
| If disabled | Caller cache headers are ignored, and every caller is served by the cache rules. Recommended. |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value = 1: Any caller, including an abusive one, can force every request to reach Roblox by adding one header. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings` |
| Related settings | `cache_enabled` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 0 |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Migration from v1 imports this value only when it differs from the v1 default (0). |

### `cache_serve_throttled`

When a caller is over its rate limit, answer from a fresh cached copy (if one exists) instead of refusing with 429. Roblox never sees these requests either way, so this only decides how strict the limit feels to the caller.

| Field | Value |

| If disabled | Throttled callers get the normal throttle refusal even when a fresh copy is cached, so the limit is strict. |

| Notes | Migration from v1 imports this value only when it differs from the v1 default (0). |

### `cache_negative_429`

When Roblox answers a request with 429 (rate limited), store a marker for that exact request until the cooldown ends. Until then the request is answered from a stale copy, or with 429 and Retry-After, without contacting Roblox; the marker itself is never served as content.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Remember Roblox 429s per request |
| Group | Cache |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 1 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Repeat requests for a rate-limited request never reach Roblox during its cooldown; callers get stale data or a 429 telling them when to retry. |
| If disabled | Only the endpoint and host cooldowns hold calls back, so repeats of the same request can reach Roblox again (for example through another egress path) and earn new 429s. |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value = 0: Callers can re-trigger Roblox 429s for a request that was just rate-limited, which lengthens Roblox's limits on Roxy. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `cache#settings` |
| Related settings | `cache_stale_seconds`, `cooldown_default_s`, `cache_error_ttl_seconds` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

Each rule is shown and editable on the Cache page.

| If disabled | Those endpoints fall back to cache_ttl_seconds, so static data such as universe ids is refetched far more often, and presence may be cached longer than it should. |

### `swr_max_inflight`

The most stale-while-revalidate background refreshes one worker runs at the same time. Each refresh is one upstream call made after a caller was already served a slightly old copy.

| Field | Value |

| Group | Cache |

| Default | 50 refreshes |
| Range | 0 to 1000 refreshes |
| Unit | refreshes |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Expired popular entries are refreshed sooner under load, but more upstream calls can run at once (they still pass through the rate buckets). |
| If lowered | Fewer refreshes run at once, so some entries stay old a little longer while they wait for a slot. 0 turns background refreshes off, so expired entries are refetched while the caller waits. |

<a id="group-throttling"></a>

## Throttling

### `allowed_requests_per_minute`

How many requests one client IP address may make in each throttle window before Roxy refuses it with 429 Too Many Requests. The window length is the separate 'Throttle window length' setting, so the default is 10 requests per 50 seconds, not per minute.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Requests per window |
| Group | Throttling |
| Type | int |
| Default | 10 requests per window |
| Range | 1 to 100000 requests per window |
| Unit | requests per window |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Callers may send more before they are throttled, which is friendlier to busy game servers but lets one IP put more load on Roxy and take a bigger share of the upstream budget that every caller shares. |
| If lowered | Callers are refused with 429 sooner. Fairer to everyone else, but games whose servers share one IP address may start seeing throttles at busy times; the THROTTLE-TUNE recommendation reports when legitimate clients are being throttled. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value >= 1000: At 1000 or more requests per window the per-IP limit barely limits anything, so a single client can take most of the upstream budget and push Roxy toward Roblox rate limits. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#throttle` |
| Related settings | `throttle_reset_duration`, `throttle_window_mode`, `throttle_count_cache_hits`, `flood_limit_per_minute`, `place_limit_per_minute`, `spam_rate_threshold`, `cache_serve_throttled` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `THROTTLE-TUNE`, `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 10 requests per window |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | The key keeps its v1 name for compatibility even though the window is 'Throttle window length' seconds long. The public home page shows the live value. |

### `throttle_reset_duration`

Length of the per-IP throttle window in seconds, and also the base penalty: a throttled client waits this long times the multiplier of its strike ladder rung. In smooth pacing (gcra) mode the allowance refills evenly over this window, so 10 per 50 seconds means a burst of 10, then one new request every 5 seconds.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Throttle window length |
| Group | Throttling |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 50 seconds |
| Range | 1 to 86400 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | The same allowance is spread over more time, so the average allowed rate drops, and every penalty gets longer (with 300 seconds, an x8 ladder rung means a 40 minute wait). |
| If lowered | The allowance refills faster, so the average allowed rate rises, and penalties get shorter, which makes the strike ladder a weaker deterrent. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#throttle` |
| Related settings | `allowed_requests_per_minute`, `throttle_window_mode`, `stale_ip_duration`, `throttle_escalation_enabled`, `throttle_strike_decay_seconds` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `THROTTLE-TUNE` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 50 seconds |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

### `throttle_window_mode`

Chooses how the per-IP limit counts requests over time. Both modes allow the same average rate; smooth pacing removes the doubled burst a fixed window allows at its edges.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Window algorithm |
| Group | Throttling |
| Type | enum |
| Default | "gcra" |
| Range | one of `fixed`, `gcra` |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#throttle` |
| Related settings | `allowed_requests_per_minute`, `throttle_reset_duration` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | New in v2. v1 always behaved like 'fixed'. |

Options:

- `fixed` (Fixed window (v1)): The v1 behavior: every client gets its full allowance at the start of each window. A client can spend one allowance at the end of a window and another right after it starts again, so short bursts of up to twice the limit get through.
- `gcra` (Smooth pacing (recommended)): A client may burst up to the limit at once, then earns one new request every (window length / limit) seconds. There is no window edge to exploit, and a client sending at exactly the limit rate is never refused. GCRA, the generic cell rate algorithm, is the standard way to pace requests evenly.

### `throttle_count_cache_hits`

Whether a request answered from Roxy's own cache uses up part of the caller's per-IP allowance. Cache hits cost Roblox nothing, so by default only requests that need a fetch from Roblox count. The flood limit still counts every request.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Count cache hits toward the per-IP limit |
| Group | Throttling |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 0 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Every request counts, cached or not (the v1 behavior), so callers polling popular, already cached endpoints get 429s for answers that never touched Roblox. |
| If disabled | Only requests that are not served from cache count, so callers reading cached data keep working; the flood limit (300 per minute by default) still caps how fast one IP can send. |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#throttle` |
| Related settings | `allowed_requests_per_minute`, `flood_limit_per_minute`, `cache_enabled`, `cache_serve_throttled` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Owner decision D10. In v1 every request counted, cached or not. |

### `stale_ip_duration`

How long Roxy keeps the throttle record of a client IP that has stopped sending requests. After this much idle time the record is deleted, which keeps the tracking table small. Clients that still carry strikes are kept until their strikes decay.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Forget idle clients after |
| Group | Throttling |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 60 seconds (1m) |
| Range | 1 to 86400 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Idle clients are remembered longer, so a client that pauses and comes back still has its partly used allowance counted; the tracking table holds more rows (it is capped at 200,000). |
| If lowered | Records are pruned sooner and the table stays smaller. Keep it at or above the throttle window length: if it is shorter, a client that pauses briefly comes back with a full allowance early. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#throttle` |
| Related settings | `throttle_reset_duration`, `throttle_strike_decay_seconds` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 60 seconds (1m) |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

### `throttle_escalation_enabled`

Each time a client trips the per-IP limit it gets a strike, and each strike moves it up a ladder of longer penalties (by default 1, 2, 4 and 8 times the window length, each rung with its own message). Repeat offenders then wait longer than a client that was too fast just once.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Escalating throttles (strike ladder) |
| Group | Throttling |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 1 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Repeat offenders get progressively longer throttles and the rung messages; strikes fade again with good behavior (see 'Strike decay time'). |
| If disabled | Every throttle lasts exactly one window length however often the client has been throttled, so a script that simply waits out each penalty is never slowed further. Strikes are still counted and shown on the strike board but do not lengthen penalties. |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#throttle` |
| Related settings | `throttle_strike_decay_seconds`, `throttle_strike_on_retry`, `throttle_reset_duration` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 1 |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | The ladder rungs (multiplier, message, action) are edited on the same card. |

### `throttle_strike_decay_seconds`

How long a client must go without a new strike to lose one strike and drop one rung of the ladder. 0 means strikes never fade on their own; an admin can still forgive them on the strike board.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Strike decay time |
| Group | Throttling |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 1800 seconds (30m) |
| Range | 0 to 604800 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Strikes last longer, so a client that misbehaves again within that time lands on a higher rung with a longer penalty. A legitimate caller that tripped the limit once also carries the strike longer. |
| If lowered | Strikes are forgiven sooner, so occasional offenders return to the plain penalty quickly, but a patient abuser can wait out the ladder and start again at the bottom. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value = 0: Strikes never decay, so a legitimate caller that tripped the limit a few times stays on the top rung (the longest penalty) until an admin forgives it by hand. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#throttle` |
| Related settings | `throttle_escalation_enabled`, `throttle_strike_on_retry`, `stale_ip_duration` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 1800 seconds (30m) |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

### `throttle_strike_on_retry`

Whether a client that keeps sending requests while it is already throttled earns an extra strike. At most one extra strike is added per window, so one impatient retry is not punished over and over.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Retrying while throttled adds a strike |
| Group | Throttling |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 1 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Clients that ignore the Retry-After header and keep hammering while throttled climb the ladder faster and wait longer; clients that wait politely are not affected. |
| If disabled | Requests sent while throttled are refused but add no strikes (the v1 behavior), so a script retrying in a tight loop pays nothing extra. |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#throttle` |

### `user_agent_rules_enabled`

Master switch for the User-Agent rules, which rate limit clients by the User-Agent header they send (a burst allowance or a minimum gap between requests). Each rule has its own switch too; this one stops or starts all of them at once.

| Group | Throttling |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 1 |

| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Every enabled User-Agent rule is enforced, in order, and the first matching rule applies. |
| If disabled | No User-Agent rule is enforced, including rules that hold back known abusive scripts; their traffic is limited only by the normal per-IP limit. Useful as a quick off switch when a rule catches legitimate callers. |

| High risk when | none |

<a id="group-abuse"></a>

## Abuse detection

### `flood_limit_per_minute`

An absolute ceiling on requests per minute from one client IP, counting every request including the ones answered from cache. It is a safety net against raw floods that normal callers should never reach; requests over it are refused with reason flood.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Flood limit per IP |
| Group | Abuse detection |
| Type | int |
| Default | 300 requests per minute |
| Range | 10 to 100000 requests per minute |
| Unit | requests per minute |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | The flood guard triggers later, so one client can push more requests per minute into Roxy (cache lookups, logging and database work) before being cut off. |
| If lowered | Floods are cut off sooner. Keep it well above the per-IP limit's rate (12 per minute with the defaults) and above what a busy game server reading cached data sends, or legitimate callers will be refused. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value >= 10000: At 10,000 or more per minute (over 160 requests per second from one IP) the flood guard no longer protects Roxy's workers and databases from a single flooding client. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#limits` |
| Related settings | `allowed_requests_per_minute`, `throttle_count_cache_hits`, `ipv6_limit_prefix`, `spam_rate_threshold` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | New in v2. nginx applies a cheaper, more generous limit in front of this one. |

### `ipv6_limit_prefix`

Per-IP limits treat all IPv6 addresses that share this many leading bits as one client, so by default every address in one /64 network shares one allowance. A home or server connection is usually given a whole /64, which holds billions of addresses, so limiting single IPv6 addresses would be easy to dodge.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | IPv6 grouping prefix |
| Group | Abuse detection |
| Type | int |
| Default | 64 prefix length (bits) |
| Range | 48 to 128 prefix length (bits) |
| Unit | prefix length (bits) |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Finer grouping. At 128 every single IPv6 address gets its own allowance, so a client can escape its limits just by switching to another address in its own network. |
| If lowered | Coarser grouping. At 48, about 65,000 /64 networks share one allowance, so unrelated users of the same provider can throttle each other. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value > 64: Most IPv6 users control at least a /64, so a finer prefix lets one client rotate addresses and get a fresh allowance each time. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#limits` |
| Related settings | `allowed_requests_per_minute`, `flood_limit_per_minute`, `place_limit_key` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | IPv4 addresses are always limited one address at a time. |

### `place_limit_enabled`

Whether Roxy enforces a request limit per Roblox experience (place), identified by the Roblox-Id header that game servers send. One experience can run on hundreds of server IPs, so per-IP limits cannot bound it, but this limit can. Off by default: Roxy only measures places and recommends.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Enforce per-experience limits |
| Group | Abuse detection |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 0 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Requests over the per-experience limit are refused with 429 and reason place_limit. Experiences share upstream capacity more fairly, but a large legitimate game can be refused at peak times; check Clients > Places before turning this on. |
| If disabled | Places are only measured. The PLACE-HEAVY recommendation still tells you when one experience takes a large share of upstream calls. |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#places`, `clients#places` |
| Related settings | `place_limit_per_minute`, `place_limit_key`, `allowed_requests_per_minute` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `PLACE-HEAVY`, `THROTTLE-TUNE` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Owner decision D11. Place ids are claims any caller can forge; see 'Place limit counts by'. |

How many requests per minute one experience (place) may send through Roxy while per-experience limits are on, counted across all of its servers as set by 'Place limit counts by'.

### `place_limit_key`

Decides what one per-experience budget belongs to. Place ids come from a header any caller can set, so counting them together with the caller's network stops a forger from exhausting a real game's budget.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Place limit counts by |
| Group | Abuse detection |
| Type | enum |
| Default | "place_prefix" |
| Range | one of `place`, `place_prefix` |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value = place: Anyone can forge a game's Roblox-Id header and use up that game's whole budget, so its real players are refused. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#places`, `clients#places` |
| Related settings | `place_limit_enabled`, `place_limit_per_minute`, `ipv6_limit_prefix` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

Options:

- `place` (Claimed place id only): One shared budget per place id. The Roblox-Id header is a claim anyone can forge, so an attacker who sends a real game's place id can use up that game's whole budget and get its players refused.
- `place_prefix` (Place id and caller network (recommended)): One budget per place id and caller network (a /24 for IPv4, a /48 for IPv6). A forger only spends the share that belongs to their own network, so a real game's servers keep theirs. A game spread across many networks gets one budget per network.

### `roblox_egress_cidrs`

IP address ranges, written in CIDR form (for example 203.0.113.0/24, meaning 256 addresses), that are known to belong to Roblox game servers. Only callers inside these ranges that also carry the game server signature (a Roblox-Id header plus a Roblox User-Agent) get bot score credit and are never auto-banned by the spam detectors. With the list empty, the signature alone earns nothing, because it is easy to forge.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Roblox game server IP ranges |
| Group | Abuse detection |
| Type | list[cidr] |
| Default | empty list |
| Range | any |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | Adding ranges trusts more callers as game servers: they get a lower bot score and immunity from automatic spam bans. A range that is too broad or wrong shields any abuser inside it. |
| If lowered | Removing ranges trusts fewer callers. With an empty list no caller gets game server credit, so real game servers are scored like any other client and can be auto-banned once the spam detectors are armed. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#places`, `clients#places`, `protection#bot` |
| Related settings | `bot_weight_no_roblox_signature`, `spam_dry_run`, `place_limit_key` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | 200 |
| Item max length | 49 |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Add only ranges Roblox itself documents; never add a range because one caller asked for it. |

### `ban_disguise_as_throttle`

When on, a banned client gets the same 429 Too Many Requests answer a throttled client gets, instead of 403 'Access denied.' with the header Roxy-Refusal: banned. Abusers who believe they are only throttled tend to wait instead of moving to a new IP address.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Disguise bans as throttles |
| Group | Abuse detection |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 1 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Banned clients cannot tell they are banned, so they are less likely to switch IPs to escape. A legitimate caller banned by mistake cannot tell either, so check the bans list when someone reports endless 429s. |
| If disabled | Banned clients get 403 'Access denied.' with Roxy-Refusal: banned. Clearer for a caller banned by mistake, but it teaches abusers to switch IPs. |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#bans` |
| Related settings | `tarpit_on_ban` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

### `bot_score_block_threshold`

Refuse requests from clients whose bot score (0 to 100, higher means more bot-like) is above this value, with reason bot_score. 0 turns blocking off; the score is then only shown and used by the detectors and recommendations.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Block clients above bot score |
| Group | Abuse detection |
| Type | int |
| Default | 0 score (0 to 100) |
| Range | 0 to 100 score (0 to 100) |
| Unit | score (0 to 100) |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Only very bot-like clients are blocked, so fewer legitimate callers are caught. |
| If lowered | More clients are blocked. Low values catch ordinary game servers: with the default weights, a client without a trusted game server signature already starts at 15 points before any bad behavior. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | none |

| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Off by default: the score never blocks on its own unless this is set (plan 10.7). |

Signal: 1 when the User-Agent is a programming library (python-requests, curl, Go-http-client) or is empty, otherwise 0. The score is the weighted average of the seven signals times 100, so a weight only matters relative to the other six.

Signal: the number of probe requests (paths like .env or wp-login, unsafe characters, non-Roblox URLs) from the client in the last 24 hours divided by 5, capped at 1. The score is the weighted average of the seven signals times 100, so a weight only matters relative to the other six.

### `bot_weight_cache_busting`

How much this signal counts in a client's bot score (0 to 100, higher means more bot-like). Signal: the share of unique query values over the client's last 200 requests, from 0 to 1. Unique values on every request (a random number or timestamp) defeat Roxy's cache. The score is the weighted average of the seven signals times 100, so a weight only matters relative to the other six.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Bot score weight: cache busting |
| Group | Abuse detection |
| Type | int |
| Default | 5 weight |
| Range | 0 to 100 weight |
| Unit | weight |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Clients whose queries are always different score higher, including ones that look up many ids. |
| If lowered | Cache busting matters less to the score; 0 ignores it (the SPAM-BUST detector still watches it). |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#bot`, `clients#client-score` |

### `challenge_enabled`

| If disabled | No challenge page; bot-like browsers are handled only by limits, rules and bans. |

### `challenge_difficulty_bits`

How hard the proof-of-work puzzle is: the browser must find a SHA-256 hash that starts with this many zero bits. Each extra bit doubles the average solve time; 18 bits takes about 0.3 seconds on a laptop.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Challenge difficulty |
| Group | Abuse detection |
| Type | int |
| Default | 18 bits |
| Range | 10 to 26 bits |
| Unit | bits |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Each pass costs scrapers more computing time, but real visitors wait longer: about 1.2 seconds at 20 bits and about 20 seconds at 24 bits on a laptop, and much longer on a phone. |
| If lowered | Puzzles solve faster, which is kinder to visitors but makes passing cheap for scrapers (12 bits takes a few milliseconds). |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value >= 24: About 20 seconds per solve on a laptop and over a minute on a phone, so many real visitors give up before the page loads. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |

### `spam_rate_threshold`

SPAM-RATE fires when a client's average request rate over the window is more than this multiple of the per-IP limit rate. With the default limit of 10 requests per 50 seconds, 5 means more than about 1 request per second kept up for the whole window (over 600 requests in 10 minutes).

| Group | Abuse detection |

| Default | 5 times the per-IP limit rate |

| Step | 0.5 |
| If raised | Only heavier floods are caught (at 10, about 2 requests per second sustained), with fewer false positives from busy but legitimate callers. |
| If lowered | Lighter sustained floods are caught; at 1 or 2, a busy game server that sits just above its limit can be flagged. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |

### `spam_rate_action`

What SPAM-RATE does when a client crosses its threshold. Whatever is chosen, detectors never ban places (place ids can be forged) and never auto-ban trusted Roblox game servers.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Sustained rate action |
| Group | Abuse detection |
| Type | enum |
| Default | "ban" |
| Range | one of `ban`, `strike`, `tarpit`, `recommend` |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#spam-rate` |
| Related settings | `spam_rate_enabled`, `spam_rate_threshold`, `spam_rate_window_s`, `spam_rate_ban_minutes`, `spam_rate_ban_max_minutes`, `spam_enabled`, `spam_dry_run`, `ban_disguise_as_throttle`, `roblox_egress_cidrs`, `tarpit_on_spam`, `allowed_requests_per_minute`, `throttle_reset_duration`, `flood_limit_per_minute` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `ABUSE-SPAM` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

Options:

- `ban` (Temporary IP ban): Bans the client's IP for the first ban length; each repeat offense within 30 days doubles the ban, up to the ban cap. While dry run is on this only logs 'would have banned'. Trusted Roblox game servers get a strike instead, and places are never banned.
- `strike` (Add a throttle strike): Refuses the offending requests and adds one throttle strike, so the client's next penalties on the strike ladder are longer. Nobody is banned.
- `tarpit` (Hold the refusals): Refuses the offending requests after a deliberate delay (the tarpit), so a script that retries at once is slowed to one attempt per hold. Nobody is banned; the hold uses the settings on Protection > Tarpit.
- `recommend` (Recommend only): Takes no action on traffic; instead it creates an ABUSE-SPAM recommendation naming the client for you to review.

### `spam_rate_ban_minutes`

How long the first automatic ban from SPAM-RATE lasts when its action is ban. Each repeat offense within 30 days doubles the ban, up to the ban cap. 0 means no ban length is set, which is only valid while the action is not ban.

| Unit | minutes |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Harsher: offenders stay out longer after a first offense, and a client banned by mistake is locked out longer too. |
| If lowered | Lighter: offenders return sooner; repeat offenses still double the ban up to the cap. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |

The longest a SPAM-RATE ban can grow to. Each repeat offense within 30 days doubles the previous ban until it reaches this cap. With the defaults a client's bans go 60 minutes, 120 minutes, 240 minutes, and so on, up to 10080 minutes (7 days).

| Unit | minutes |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Persistent repeat offenders can be banned for longer, up to 10080 minutes (7 days). |
| If lowered | Even persistent offenders return sooner; setting it equal to the first ban turns escalation off. |

| If disabled | n/a |

### `spam_refused_threshold`

SPAM-REFUSED fires when more than this many of one client's requests are refused within the window (by default more than 200 refusals in 10 minutes).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Ignored refusals threshold |
| Group | Abuse detection |
| Type | int |
| Default | 200 refused requests |
| Range | 10 to 100000 refused requests |
| Unit | refused requests |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Clients may ignore more refusals before the detector acts, with fewer false positives from clients that have clumsy retry logic. |
| If lowered | Clients that ignore 429s and keep retrying are caught sooner, but a legitimate client with an aggressive retry loop may be caught too. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |

### `spam_refused_action`

What SPAM-REFUSED does when a client crosses its threshold. Whatever is chosen, detectors never ban places (place ids can be forged) and never auto-ban trusted Roblox game servers.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Ignored refusals action |
| Group | Abuse detection |
| Type | enum |
| Default | "ban" |
| Range | one of `ban`, `strike`, `tarpit`, `recommend` |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#spam-refused` |
| Related settings | `spam_refused_enabled`, `spam_refused_threshold`, `spam_refused_window_s`, `spam_refused_ban_minutes`, `spam_refused_ban_max_minutes`, `spam_enabled`, `spam_dry_run`, `ban_disguise_as_throttle`, `roblox_egress_cidrs`, `tarpit_on_spam`, `throttle_strike_on_retry`, `tarpit_on_spam` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `ABUSE-SPAM` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | none |

Options:

- `ban` (Temporary IP ban): Bans the client's IP for the first ban length; each repeat offense within 30 days doubles the ban, up to the ban cap. While dry run is on this only logs 'would have banned'. Trusted Roblox game servers get a strike instead, and places are never banned.
- `strike` (Add a throttle strike): Refuses the offending requests and adds one throttle strike, so the client's next penalties on the strike ladder are longer. Nobody is banned.
- `tarpit` (Hold the refusals): Refuses the offending requests after a deliberate delay (the tarpit), so a script that retries at once is slowed to one attempt per hold. Nobody is banned; the hold uses the settings on Protection > Tarpit.
- `recommend` (Recommend only): Takes no action on traffic; instead it creates an ABUSE-SPAM recommendation naming the client for you to review.

| If disabled | Clients trying to push account cookies through Roxy are refused each time but never banned. |

Options:

- `ban` (Temporary IP ban): Bans the client's IP for the first ban length; each repeat offense within 30 days doubles the ban, up to the ban cap. While dry run is on this only logs 'would have banned'. Trusted Roblox game servers get a strike instead, and places are never banned.
- `strike` (Add a throttle strike): Refuses the offending requests and adds one throttle strike, so the client's next penalties on the strike ladder are longer. Nobody is banned.
- `tarpit` (Hold the refusals): Refuses the offending requests after a deliberate delay (the tarpit), so a script that retries at once is slowed to one attempt per hold. Nobody is banned; the hold uses the settings on Protection > Tarpit.
- `recommend` (Recommend only): Takes no action on traffic; instead it creates an ABUSE-SPAM recommendation naming the client for you to review.

### `spam_bust_enabled`

Turns the SPAM-BUST detector on or off. SPAM-BUST watches for cache busting: a client adding a query value that changes on almost every request (a random number or a timestamp), which defeats Roxy's cache and sends every request on to Roblox.

| Default | 1 |

| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | SPAM-BUST checks every client and, when the threshold is crossed, takes its action (by default: recommend only). Bans are only logged while dry run is on. |
| If disabled | Cache busting clients are no longer reported, although the CACHE-KEYSPLIT recommendation can still spot a busting parameter from cache statistics. |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |

### `spam_dist_threshold`

SPAM-DIST fires when more than this many different client IPs, all with one User-Agent, together send over 1,000 requests to one endpoint template within the window (by default more than 50 IPs in 5 minutes).

### `spam_dist_action`

What SPAM-DIST does when a client crosses its threshold. Whatever is chosen, detectors never ban places (place ids can be forged) and never auto-ban trusted Roblox game servers.

Options:

- `ban` (Temporary IP ban): Bans the client's IP for the first ban length; each repeat offense within 30 days doubles the ban, up to the ban cap. While dry run is on this only logs 'would have banned'. Trusted Roblox game servers get a strike instead, and places are never banned.
- `strike` (Add a throttle strike): Refuses the offending requests and adds one throttle strike, so the client's next penalties on the strike ladder are longer. Nobody is banned.
- `tarpit` (Hold the refusals): Refuses the offending requests after a deliberate delay (the tarpit), so a script that retries at once is slowed to one attempt per hold. Nobody is banned; the hold uses the settings on Protection > Tarpit.
- `recommend` (Recommend only): Takes no action on traffic; instead it creates an ABUSE-DIST recommendation suggesting a User-Agent rule with global scope, or a temporary emergency limit (throttle-all).

### `spam_dist_ban_minutes`

How long the first automatic ban from SPAM-DIST lasts when its action is ban. Each repeat offense within 30 days doubles the ban, up to the ban cap. 0 means no ban length is set, which is only valid while the action is not ban.

<a id="group-tarpit"></a>

## Tarpit

### `tarpit_enabled`

Master switch for the tarpit. A tarpit makes a caller that Roxy has already decided to refuse wait before the refusal is sent, so a script retrying in a loop can only make one attempt per hold. It never delays a request Roxy serves, and never applies to clients on the bypass list.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Tarpit enabled |
| Group | Tarpit |
| Type | bool |
| Default | 1 |
| Range | 0 (off) or 1 (on) |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Refusals in the categories switched on below are held, up to the fleet-wide cap, before they are sent. Each hold costs Roxy one idle task and one connection and costs the abuser seconds of waiting. |
| If disabled | Every refusal is sent instantly, whatever the category switches say. Abusive scripts can retry as fast as their network allows, which raises load on Roxy and fills the logs faster. |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#tarpit` |
| Related settings | `tarpit_default_type`, `tarpit_min_seconds`, `tarpit_max_seconds`, `tarpit_max_concurrent` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `TARPIT-TUNE`, `ABUSE-SPAM`, `FILTER-ADD` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 1 |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Fails closed (plan C7): if the shared hold counter in hot.db cannot be read or written, Roxy does not hold and sends the refusal instantly. The caller always receives the same refusal it would have received without the tarpit; only the timing changes. |

### `tarpit_min_seconds`

The shortest time a held refusal waits before it is sent. Each hold picks a random length between this and the longest hold, so a script cannot learn the exact delay and set its timeout just below it.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Shortest hold |
| Group | Tarpit |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 8 seconds |
| Range | 0 to 55 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Every held caller waits at least this long, so abusers lose more time per attempt. Holds last longer, so more of them overlap and the hold cap is reached sooner (later refusals are then sent instantly). |
| If lowered | Holds can be very short, which weakens the tarpit (at 0 some holds end almost at once). Fewer connections are tied up at any moment. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#tarpit` |
| Related settings | `tarpit_max_seconds`, `tarpit_max_concurrent`, `request_deadline_s` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `TARPIT-TUNE` |
| Auto-apply bounds | 2 to 15 |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 8 seconds |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Must be less than or equal to the longest hold (checked when you save). Imported from v1 only if you had changed it (plan 18.3). |

### `tarpit_max_seconds`

The longest time a held refusal waits before it is sent. Together with the shortest hold it sets the random range each hold is picked from.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Longest hold |
| Group | Tarpit |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 20 seconds |
| Range | 1 to 55 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Abusers wait longer per attempt on average. Each hold keeps its connection open longer, so more holds overlap and the hold cap fills sooner; refusals beyond the cap are sent instantly and counted as skipped. |
| If lowered | Holds end sooner and cost the abuser less time; fewer connections are held at once. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#tarpit` |
| Related settings | `tarpit_min_seconds`, `tarpit_max_concurrent`, `tarpit_slot_grace_s`, `request_deadline_s` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `TARPIT-TUNE` |
| Auto-apply bounds | 5 to 40 |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 20 seconds |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Must be at least the shortest hold and at most the request deadline minus 2 seconds (checked when you save). The 55 second ceiling keeps every hold inside the request deadline, so nginx and the server never time the request out before Roxy sends the refusal. Imported from v1 only if you had changed it. |

### `tarpit_max_concurrent`

The most refusals the whole fleet may hold at the same time, counted across every worker process. Past this number a refusal is sent instantly and counted as skipped, so the tarpit can never tie up all of Roxy's connections. The cap actually used can be lower: see the connection budget and the share of it the tarpit may use.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Most holds at once |
| Group | Tarpit |
| Type | int |
| Default | 50 holds |
| Range | 0 to 500 holds |
| Unit | holds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | More abusive requests can be held at once during a large attack. Holds are cheap (an idle task each), but every hold uses two nginx connections, one to the caller and one to Roxy. |
| If lowered | More refusals skip the tarpit and are sent instantly. 0 stops all holding while leaving the category switches as they are. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#tarpit` |
| Related settings | `tarpit_max_capacity_fraction`, `tarpit_connection_budget`, `tarpit_slot_grace_s` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `TARPIT-TUNE` |
| Auto-apply bounds | 10 to 200 |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 6 holds |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | v1 counted request threads (default 6, at most 64) because a held request occupied a whole thread. v2 holds are idle async tasks, so the default is 50. Never imported from v1 because the meaning changed (plan 18.3). The Protection > Tarpit card shows the effective cap and which limit clamped it. |

### `tarpit_max_capacity_fraction`

A safety clamp on the hold cap, written as a share of the connection budget (0.25 means a quarter). The cap actually enforced is the smaller of 'Most holds at once' and this share of the connection budget, rounded down, so a typo in the hold cap cannot let held refusals use up the connections real callers need.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Share of connection budget the tarpit may use |
| Group | Tarpit |
| Type | float |
| Default | 0.25 fraction |
| Range | 0.05 to 0.5 fraction |
| Unit | fraction |
| Step | 0.01 |
| If raised | Held refusals may use more of the connection budget, leaving less room for real callers while an attack is being held. |
| If lowered | The clamp engages sooner: fewer holds at once and more instant refusals. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | value > 0.4: More than 40 percent of the connection budget could be tied up by held refusals, so an attack that triggers many holds can leave too few nginx connections for real callers. |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#tarpit` |
| Related settings | `tarpit_connection_budget`, `tarpit_max_concurrent` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `TARPIT-TUNE`, `SEC-DEFAULTS` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 0.5 fraction |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Was the v1 constant TARPIT_MAX_CAPACITY_FRACTION (0.5 of the request threads, workers x threads). v2 holds do not occupy threads, so the scarce resource is nginx connections; the base is now the connection budget and the default 0.25. With the defaults the clamp allows 1000 holds, so the hold cap of 50 is what applies. |

### `tarpit_connection_budget`

How many held requests nginx (the web server in front of Roxy) can carry at once, already halved because every held request uses two nginx connections: one to the caller and one to Roxy. Roxy cannot read nginx's configuration, so this setting tells it. The default 4000 matches 2 nginx worker processes with 4096 connections each (8192 connections, halved, rounded down).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Connection budget |
| Group | Tarpit |
| Type | int |
| Default | 4000 connections |
| Range | 100 to 100000 connections |
| Unit | connections |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Allows more holds before the clamp engages. If the number is higher than nginx really supports, held refusals can use up nginx's connections and real callers are turned away by nginx itself. |
| If lowered | The clamp engages sooner, so fewer holds and more instant refusals; only the tarpit gets weaker. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | medium |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#tarpit` |
| Related settings | `tarpit_max_capacity_fraction`, `tarpit_max_concurrent` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `SYS-HEALTH-FAIL` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Replaces the v1 constant TARPIT_FALLBACK_SLOTS (16) and the thread count it stood in for. The deploy records the real nginx values in roxy.env (ROXY_NGINX_WORKER_PROCESSES and ROXY_NGINX_WORKER_CONNECTIONS), and the H-NGINX health check warns when this setting disagrees with them. Change it here after changing nginx's worker settings. |

### `tarpit_slot_grace_s`

Each hold takes a slot in a shared counter, and the slot carries an expiry time (a lease) equal to the hold length plus this grace period. If a worker process dies in the middle of a hold, its slot frees itself when the lease expires, so a crash can never use up slots permanently.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Hold slot grace period |
| Group | Tarpit |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 15 seconds |
| Range | 1 to 120 seconds |
| Unit | seconds |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Slots of a crashed worker stay taken longer, so after a crash or restart the tarpit has fewer free slots for a while. |
| If lowered | Slots of a crashed worker are reclaimed sooner, but a hold that runs late (for example on a busy server) can lose its slot early, letting the fleet briefly hold more requests than the cap. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#tarpit` |
| Related settings | `tarpit_max_concurrent`, `tarpit_max_seconds` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | 15 seconds |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Was the v1 constant TARPIT_SLOT_GRACE (15 seconds). |

### `tarpit_default_type`

How a held refusal is delayed, for every category that does not use a fixed style of its own (callers retrying inside their Retry-After always get jitter). Whatever the style, the caller ends up with the same refusal it would have received instantly.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Hold style |
| Group | Tarpit |
| Type | enum |
| Default | "hold" |
| Range | one of `hold`, `drip`, `jitter` |
| Unit | none |
| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#tarpit` |
| Related settings | `tarpit_min_seconds`, `tarpit_max_seconds`, `tarpit_drip_interval_ms`, `tarpit_jitter_min_ms`, `tarpit_jitter_max_ms` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | `TARPIT-TUNE` |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | New in v2; v1 always used the hold style. |

Options:

- `hold` (Hold): Wait a random time between the shortest and longest hold, then send the normal refusal. Costs Roxy one idle task and one connection per hold; the caller waits the full time. Best for probes and header rule hits. This is how v1 worked.
- `drip` (Drip): Send the response headers at once, then the body one byte per drip interval until the hold ends. Clients that read the body are held for the whole time and some give up and time out. Best for scrapers that ignore status codes. nginx buffering and compression are switched off for these responses so the bytes really trickle out.
- `jitter` (Jitter): Add only a short random delay, between the jitter minimum and maximum, before the refusal. Costs almost nothing and breaks tight retry loops without holding connections for long. Best for ordinary rate limit refusals.

### `tarpit_drip_interval_ms`

For the drip style: the gap, in milliseconds, between the single bytes of the response body that Roxy sends while a hold lasts.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Label | Drip interval |
| Group | Tarpit |
| Type | duration |
| Default | 1000 ms (1s) |
| Range | 100 to 10000 ms |
| Unit | ms |
| Step | 1 |
| If raised | Bytes trickle out more slowly, so Roxy makes fewer tiny writes; a client with a short read timeout is more likely to give up, which ends its hold early. |
| If lowered | Bytes arrive faster, so clients are less likely to time out and stay held for the whole hold; Roxy makes more tiny writes per held request. |
| If enabled | n/a |
| If disabled | n/a |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
| Dashboard | `protection#tarpit` |
| Related settings | `tarpit_default_type`, `tarpit_max_seconds` |
| Related rules | none |
| Related recommendations | none |
| Auto-apply bounds | none |
| Sensitive | no |
| Since | 2.0 |
| v1 key | same key, or new in v2 |
| v1 default | none |
| Max length | none |
| Item max length | none |
| Item range | none |
| Pending owner verification | no |
| Notes | Only used when the hold style is drip. |

| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Callers caught by your header rules wait for their refusal. These are almost never real users, because you wrote the rule to match them. |
| If disabled | Header rule refusals are sent instantly, so a fingerprinted script can retry at full speed. |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |

Hold refusals for requests that are not valid Roblox API calls at all, such as a URL that is not a Roblox host or a malformed or unsafe path. This is what vulnerability scanners send; a real caller never does. Only applies while the tarpit master switch is on.

| Step | none |
| If raised | n/a |
| If lowered | n/a |
| If enabled | Scanners and junk requests wait for their refusal, which slows down automated probing. |
| If disabled | Probe refusals are sent instantly, so a scanner can try thousands of paths quickly. |
| Risk | low |
| High risk when | none |
| Applies | live (hot reload fleet-wide within about a second) |
